# Research Record: Directed Realization and the Yang–Mills Gap

This record preserves the constructions, source checks and failure conditions of the Yang–Mills inquiry. The current priority is [[contemporary-puzzles/yang-mills-mass-gap/directed-realization-and-foundational-restart|directed realization]]: identify a law that jointly selects the observable carrier, positive form and dynamics. The existing Wilson estimates remain conditional downstream results; they do not yet select that law or complete the four-dimensional continuum theory. External deep research and further local-estimate repairs are paused for this foundational audit.

[[contemporary-puzzles/yang-mills-mass-gap/clay-contract-and-scale-assumptions|The attached-statement audit]] separates Clay's required observable return from optional construction methods and from the stronger programme of deriving spacetime itself. The earlier long opening is preserved verbatim in [[junk-drawer/pre-foundational-restart-opening|the opening archive]]; the historical investigations below remain intact.

## Research scope and success condition

The target is not a numerical coincidence or a reinterpretation of a known Hamiltonian inequality. Success requires:

- a carrier and vacuum constructed without using the desired low spectrum;
- physical distinction maps fixed by causal, gauge, topological, or descent data;
- a uniform lower-frame or Poincare estimate on the full vacuum complement;
- an independently calibrated energy, inverse-length, or clock-rate scale;
- a derived dimensionless form comparison between distinction and physical energy after the yardstick is fixed;
- a typed separation of descent, nonfaithful forgetting, outcome selection, record formation, and spectral coercivity;
- control through infinite volume and continuum removal; and
- exact recovery of the pure-Yang--Mills observable net, vacuum state, translation representation, and energy form, or their controlled recovery in a decoupling limit; restriction to a gauge-observable subalgebra of a larger theory is not enough.

Selection of $SU(3)$, Standard Model matter, or numerical MeV values is outside the compatibility claim. The Clay statement is indexed by every compact simple gauge group.

## Research plan executed

1. Audit the exact mathematical statement and its continuum obligations.
2. Audit the workspace's carrier-first, flux-Dirichlet, conditional-expectation, Type III/core, Hessian, knot, grain, and compatibility constructions.
3. Search primary literature for the operator-algebra, Dirichlet-form, frame, RG, reconstruction, and local-covariance components.
4. Build a gap matrix separating what each clue supplies from what it cannot supply.
5. Formulate one sufficient theorem with a non-circularity test and a continuum transfer theorem.
6. Type the proposed measurement bridge as separate pointing, information-defect, and spectral-gap arrows.
7. State exact extension, approximate effective recovery, and strong emergence contracts separately.
8. Solve the finite product-Haar frame problem exactly, restrict it to the gauge-invariant spin-network carrier, and identify a local-mixing theorem that could transfer the bound to the interacting vacuum without global density comparison.
9. Construct the finite-regulator Euclidean time-slice sewing map, identify its boundary residue and Gaussian Dirichlet-to-Neumann member, and derive the direct inheritance of bulk functional inequalities by the vacuum slice marginal.
10. Separate causal boundedness from UV regulation, replace the single-patch picture by an atlas of overlapping patches, distinguish record order from Euclidean and Lorentzian time, and prove the cross-carrier causal-semigroup comparison theorem in [[contemporary-puzzles/yang-mills-mass-gap/causal-patch-boundary-and-two-times|the causal-patch note]].
11. Retype mass as the Poincare Casimir of the reconstructed translation representation, separate measurement pointing and phase selection from spectral coercivity, and audit the \(G,c,\hbar\) scale triangle against the gravity-free pure Yang--Mills target in [[contemporary-puzzles/yang-mills-mass-gap/mass-as-casimir-and-realization|the realization note]].
12. Separate physical unit lines from conformal scale densities, prove the general same-carrier dilation no-gap lemma, and type inverse-Compton mass as a length dual only after the \(c,\hbar\) comparison maps are supplied.
13. Audit the horizon/entropy intuition at fixed boundary scale and formulate the no-interior Copernican route first as an exterior-equivalence reconstruction problem in [[contemporary-puzzles/yang-mills-mass-gap/horizon-saturation-and-entropic-distinction|the entropic-distinction note]]; representation-sector language is admitted only after a GNS criterion is proved.

Replace the ill-typed BKM Hessian at the global pure vacuum by a family of faithful observable-output states, pull their relative-entropy Hessians back to projective vacuum tangents, prove the finite regional-frame theorem, and test its blind directions in [[contemporary-puzzles/yang-mills-mass-gap/regional-relative-entropy-frames|the regional-frame note]].
15. Audit whether quantum Markov or modular generators can supply the missing clock rate; prove the rate-rescaling and modular no-go boundaries, and isolate paired null generators followed by a joint Poincare Casimir as the viable modular reconstruction target.
16. Audit entropic-gravity, entanglement-equilibrium, and stochastic-gravity programmes for reusable all-cut and second-variation structure in [[contemporary-puzzles/yang-mills-mass-gap/entropic-stiffness-not-entropic-force|the entropic-stiffness note]], without importing \(G\), an Unruh acceleration, or stochastic ontology.

Expand the category correction into presentation/object, formal-series/function, ultraviolet-limit/fixed-member, coefficient/spectrum, number/quantity, and cutoff/continuum distinctions; formulate scale covariance on a one-scale family rather than as a symmetry of one fixed member, and identify quantitative RG crossover as the remaining bridge.

Restrict the core wall to its logarithmic scale shadow, use the normalized dual-action derivation to prove the logistic weighted-gradient gap, strengthen the flat-partner classification so that normalizable pointing forces a positive constant, expose the projection-versus-involution normalization fork in the optional capacity/readout solder, formulate the independent incoming-density law that selects \(\nu=1/2\), and isolate the physical-to-wall Casimir analysis map as the remaining carrier bridge.

Put the scale factors on explicit Sobolev domains and compute their Fredholm classes: the probability factor is index one for every positive width, while the translation-Haar capacity-relative factor has two Fredholm phases separated by the exact incoming rate wall. Identify the post-crossing index as a possible homotopy-stable fossil while keeping its observable image and dimensional calibration open.
21. Factor the finite-regulator OS quotient through a reflection-fixed Markov separator. Separate the exact conditional-expectation unitary and the conditionally closable interface derivative from the additional interface-to-slice and boundary-to-log-scale maps, and localize the first Yang--Mills analytic bottleneck as a uniform weak-coupling cylinder functional inequality plus energy-form comparison.

Prove exact Parseval coverage, scale-translation and unitary naturality, the logistic \(\nu=1/2\) channel law, the invariant mean-center theorem for reciprocal causal directions, and the equivalence between a lower spectral edge, a ceiling on \(-\log L\), and norm-uniform effect saturation. Distinguish fixed physical thickness from adjacent-cut continuum scaling.

42. Split a pointed fact into its visible counterfactual complement and hidden antecedent fibre. Prove that the intrinsic four-dimensional normal is blind to color and \(U(1)\), while the 149-dimensional full defining-data normal is faithful; derive its branching and trace-index ratio, construct its finite-lattice Wilson holonomy response, and retain interacting-vacuum coverage, OS kinetic comparison, continuum coercivity, and dimensional calibration as separate obligations.

Treat \(F_4\to F_4/H\) as the torsor of local presentations of the oriented flag, show that compatible comparison arrows become \(H\)-valued links with the standard gauge-coordinate law, and transport the defining-data normal around plaquettes. Distinguish the flag-normal Hessian route, which still requires a field-valued physical analysis map, from the holonomy route, where the normal representation is only a response probe. Derive the projective logarithmic-transfer Rayleigh rate and Einstein area compliance, prove the one-form/quadratic-form and static-ledger/rate no-gos, and identify the two-slice edge law plus explicit ground-state unitary as the minimal rate-bearing bridge.
49. Replace the Gaussian-only marginal Schur complement by the exact nonlinear Helffer--Sjostrand formula on conditional exact one-forms. Track the negative covariance sign for \(\beta>0\), state the block-positivity requirement for operator-short language, and use the compact-manifold Hessian no-go to redirect the Yang--Mills target toward Bakry--Emery or functional inequalities.
50. Audit the integrable-\(S^6\) descent data without merging them into the octonionic stabilizer. Derive \(\chi_\ell(c)=e^{2\pi ip/12}\) from the signed winding integers; prove that every nontrivial character of \(\Delta^+(3,4,\infty)_{\mathrm{ab}}\cong C_{12}\) removes the cusp zero mode and yields a positive compact-resolvent Casimir sector; exhibit the genus-three commutator cover; and keep that theorem separate from both the unit-cokernel topology and the octonionic twisted-Dirac index \(\pm1\).
51. Locate the first natural transport from global descent to the neutral Yang--Mills carrier. The audit yields three exact boundaries and one positive bridge. First, the augmentation and every center-character construction split away from the invariant glueball block; one-form center symmetry charges lines rather than point-local glueball operators. Second, twisted finite-volume neutral thresholds scale as \(1/L\), while the controlled center-stabilized example requires a nonperturbative monopole-induced Hessian to gap the neutral dual photons. Third, global discreteness is not uniform rigidity: \(C_3*C_4\) lacks property \((T)\), and an explicit irreducible family has cusp holonomy approaching the identity. Positively, Longo's theorem and its differentiated form give \(q_{\mathrm{loss}}\leq q_B\leq(2\pi R/\hbar c)\mathfrak h\) on real localized tangents under entropy and energy-form differentiability. A spectral gap additionally requires a positive Hermitian extension with the same upper bound and a noncircular lower frame on a complex Hamiltonian form core.
52. **[IN PROGRESS]** Construct the missing first arrow. A finite Type-I member now exists: for the irreducible two-dimensional \(C_3*C_4\) family, passage to \(V\otimes\bar V\simeq\operatorname{End}(V)\) cancels scalar charge, the two cyclic conjugation averages have common range \(\mathbb CI\), and their summed restriction-loss Hessian has sharp relative floor \(1-|\cos2\theta|\) on traceless finite-algebra density tangents. Its order-odd commutator retains handedness separately. These affine paths are not localized inner-unitary QFT paths, and one centered fibre is only three-real-dimensional. The remaining task is an infinite presentation-frame family, a natural complex-linear analysis map from a gauge-invariant Yang--Mills form core, and domination of its entire summed form by one genuine regional Type-III restriction-loss form with a positive Hermitian complex-core extension. Test representation-specific closed range, relative property \((T)\), expansion, multiple-holonomy common-centralizer conditions, and nonperturbative neutral Hessians without requiring the triangle group itself to be Kazhdan. Track the relative angle uniformly through volume, deformation, and continuum removal.
53. Audit the cosmological yardstick as a whole-to-part selection law rather than a substitute for local coercivity. Classify every energy constructible from \(c,G,\hbar,H\) and dimensionless acceleration, age, entropy, integer, and logarithmic data; derive the exact common-count identities \(E_*=(6\pi^2/\gamma s_*)^{1/3}E_A\iota_A^{-2/3}=\hbar He^{\Sigma}\) and the dimensionless selector \(\Xi_m=\iota_A^{-1}(mc^2/\hbar H)^3=Gcm^3/(\pi\hbar^2H)\); compose a hypothetical width map with the Hermitian Longo comparison; and expose the live-drift and gravity-decoupling no-gos. In particular, \(\mathrm d\log\Xi_m/\mathrm dt=3\dot m/m+\epsilon H\), so a constant local mass cannot obey the live common-count identity away from an exact de Sitter branch. Split selection into a dilation-invariant shape condition for \(N_c\) and a normalization-sensitive capacity or logarithmic-depth condition for \(H_c\). The strongest present two-boundary target is a unit-ledger birth, a transverse algebraic wall crossing, and a finite depth \(\mathscr D_c=2\int_{N_b}^{N_c}\epsilon\,\mathrm dN\), giving \(H_c=[\pi c^5/(G\hbar)]^{1/2}e^{-\mathscr D_c/2}\). Under the additional centered-convex isotropic equal-volume-cell premise, derive \(R_B=(3/4\pi)^{1/3}\lambda_*\) and the dimension-three cancellation \(\Delta_E\geq\varkappa_B(\gamma s_*)^{-1/3}E_A\iota_A^{-2/3}\). The active targets are both selectors, physical cell-to-localization identification, dimensional fossil transport, and factorization of cosmological scale selection from a gravity-independent positive Yang--Mills coefficient in a fixed RG convention.
54. State the internal yardstick as a generalized rate-edge problem on one physical carrier. Compare a closed local attenuation form with the scalar cosmic clock form calibrated on that carrier, derive the conditional bound \(\Delta_E\geq\hbar\eta_{\mathrm{sol}}H_cQ_{\mathrm{rat}}\), and keep the common-count scale ratio \(Q_{\mathrm{cc}}=e^\Sigma\) separate until a composition-preserving theorem proves the sufficient comparison \(\eta_{\mathrm{sol}}Q_{\mathrm{rat}}\geq\underline C_{\mathrm{YM}}^{(\mathsf s)}C_{\mathrm{cos}}^{(\mathsf s)}Q_{\mathrm{cc}}\). Keep equality as a separate sharpness question. Prove the dilation trilemma. Then test an intrinsically normalized dualizable endocorrespondence as a discrete selector: under the explicitly conjectural log-capacity/categorical-index weld, fusion depth gives \(\log(\iota_n/\iota_b)=2n\log d\), \(H_n/H_b=d^{-n}\), and \(\int\epsilon\,\mathrm dN=n\log d\). Treat the weld, synchronization, and fixed birth section as the new load-bearing construction rather than consequences of finite-index theory.
55. Identify the exact information-theoretic object on the categorical side and split the cosmological weld. For a finite-index expectation of infinite-dimensional factors, use the dual expectation to obtain a statewise two-sided certainty budget equal to the log Kosaki index and use optimality to identify the same number as supremal one-sided Araki loss. Under a standard minimal sector realized over infinite-dimensional factors this is \(2\log d(X)\), additive under fusion. Then separate the conservative additive-entropy weld \(\iota_n-\iota_b=2n\log d\) from the stronger multiplicative effective-cell-count weld \(\iota_n/\iota_b=d^{2n}\). Only the latter gives the geometric Hubble ladder, and it additionally interprets normalized horizon area as an effective count. Derive the exact fractional horizon-ledger rate \(2\epsilon H\), the additive rate \(2\epsilon H\iota_A\), and the integer-crossing address \(\dot\nu=\epsilon H/\log d\). Audit the exchange-rate reading \(c^3/G\): division by the fractional and additive ledger rates gives inequivalent masses \(M_A/\epsilon\) and \(M_A/(\epsilon\iota_A)\), while the common-count member scales as \(M_A\iota_A^{-2/3}\), so the exponent remains a geometric input. Keep wall selection, absolute normalization, fossil transport, neutral coercivity, and the pure-gauge limit open.
56. Locate a genuine dimensionless lower edge in the exceptional categorical lane and prove that it is not index capacity in disguise. For a finite symmetric tensor-generating set, use the Vaes--Valvekens normalized fusion average and Popa--Vaes Kazhdan projection to obtain \(L\geq\kappa(1-p_{\mathrm K})\) in every admissible tube-algebra representation. Corey Jones proves this edge for the quantum-\(G_2\) category at every positive \(q\neq1\), while the classical \(q=1\) member has capacity \(2\log7>0\) but zero categorical rigidity. Correct the Q-system normalization by splitting off its compulsory tensor-unit summand. Under the explicitly conjectural cosmic weld, \(\mathscr D_c/(2n)=\log d_q(X)\) has a sharp trichotomy: below \(2n\log7\) there is no positive-real solution, equality selects the gapless classical member, and greater depth selects a unique \(|\log q|>0\). With the separate proposed one-channel birth section, the existing crossing anchors give the post-search ceiling \(n_{\max}=72\) and \(q\simeq1.017\)--\(1.018\) at that ceiling; do not identify tensor depth with the exceptional normal's dimension seventy-two without a monoidal incidence theorem. Independently, use the Sano--Friedrichs operator to show that a two-expectation edge is \(1-c_F\), and the Bakshi--Guin fixed-index family to prove that neither indices nor one fixed reducible Q-system bound it uniformly. The active physical target is an admissible categorical action on every neutral Yang--Mills energy-form tangent, with invariant image exactly the vacuum, a uniform lower frame, domination by one regional Type-III loss form, and controlled OS/Poincare and gravity-decoupling limits.
57. Test the categorical edge on the nearest physical carriers instead of assuming the exceptional-to-Yang--Mills action. Prove that restricted fundamental Wilson-character multiplication is exactly classical fusion on \(L^2(SU(3))^{\operatorname{Ad}}\), while it has no normalizable invariant GNS vector and only a formal distributional \(1\)-eigenstate/evaluation functional at the flat connection; its defect has no edge after removal of any fixed normalizable vacuum line. Decompose quantum-dimension normalization into a removable scalar plus a rescaled ordinary Wilson potential, and verify that the scalar cancels from normalized Gibbs laws and excitation-energy differences. Audit canonical Type-III hypergroup UCP actions on standard GNS space and show that their fixed subspace contains the entire smaller algebra's GNS carrier. Formulate the target surviving these audits as a family of state-preserving UCP maps on the neutral observable algebra satisfying the normalized fusion law, whose GNS fusion representation is annular/tube admissible and whose joint invariant space is exactly \(\mathbb C\Omega\); keep its regional entropy domination and physical clock calibration as separate obligations.
58. Refactor the carrier question across Hessian response, binary information geometry, and CWST. For an invariant-state UCP operation, keep its algebra action, GNS contraction, and BKM tangent contraction separate. Use the balanced binary channel to prove that GNS disagreement \(1-r\) is not information loss \(1-r^2\), with the reversible flip as the sharp counterexample. For a regular analysis map \(J\), type the pullback as \(G(v,w)=g(Jv,Jw)\), equivalently \(G^\flat=J^\vee g^\flat J\), and prove \(\operatorname{rad}G=\ker J\) when the target metric is positive definite. 59. Retype the Copernican nonunitarity claim as a theorem about arrows. A genuinely forgetful whole-to-local realization is nonfaithful and therefore cannot be an equivalence or unitary conjugation, whereas strongly continuous unitary clock evolution may remain exact inside the realized local algebra; a Stinespring dilation factors the channel but does not restore an inverse or prove a hidden reversible ontology. On localized inner-unitary paths, prove that the regional state differential has kernel equal to the centered vacuum centralizer, formulate the exact quotient-factorization test for categorical-defect domination, and require a regulator-uniform relative lower frame beyond Reeh--Schlieder density.
60. Prove the kernel-stabilizer reconstruction theorem. An upstream automorphism group descends through a quotient \(q:\mathcal A\to\mathcal A/I\) exactly when it preserves \(I\); an invariant quotient state implements the descended group by GNS unitaries, while GNS-strong continuity is a separate hypothesis before Stone's theorem supplies a generator. More generally, transformations preserving a semidefinite response descend as isometries of its radical quotient. Interpret the lifted observable symmetry as the image of the stabilizer of what the wall made invisible, while keeping the claim that loss causes local unitarity open.
61. Split the local object as a retract rather than treating the whole and local identities as identical. For \(W\xrightarrow qL\xrightarrow hW\) with \(qh=1\), prove \(\operatorname{End}(L)\cong e\operatorname{End}(W)e\), \(e=hq\). A local unitary is a unit relative to \(e\), while its whole-carrier representative kills \(\ker q\) and is noninvertible relative to \(I_W\). With a harmonic section, prove that the vertical response is removed once and the retained action is reversible.
62. Derive the local process from the wall by trace-Dirichlet descent. Define \(\check{\mathcal E}[\varphi]=\inf_{qw=\varphi}\mathcal E_W[w]\); when this form is closed and Markovian, use its Dirichlet-to-Neumann operator as the local distinction generator. Prove that \(D_q\geq\kappa(I-P_0)\) is equivalent to \(\mathcal E_W[w]\geq\kappa\|(I-P_0)qw\|^2\) on every whole lift. Isolate complete Markovianity of this infimal pushforward as the Type-III theorem target rather than assuming that an arbitrary positive Schur complement is a quantum Markov generator.
63. Test the construction on a weighted graph with a soft dangling bulk mode. Verify that the whole gap tends to zero while the boundary trace operator retains gap two, because every low-cost whole mode becomes boundary-constant. Use the same harmonic section to verify the split idempotent and the fact that a local swap is invertible in the retained corner while its whole representative is noninvertible.
64. Derive a process directly from an ordered pair of trace-preserving expectations. Prove that \(E_PE_QE_P\) is GNS-self-adjoint and UCP, that its Poissonization is a quantum Markov semigroup with generator \(I-pqp\), and that the sharp reduced edge is \(1-c_F^2\). Use the fixed-index spin-model family to prove that index or one Q-system cannot make this edge regulator-uniform, and keep the common retained range distinct from the physical vacuum.
65. Short the weighted two-wall response \(\alpha(I-P)+\beta(I-Q)\) to the \(P\)-retained carrier. Derive the exact rational response \(\alpha\beta(I-C)(\alpha I+\beta C)^{-1}\), its Friedrichs-angle floor, and the single-wall tangential no-go. In the stationary OS path-space member, identify the equal-weight short with \(\tanh(daH_T/\hbar c)\), while recording that this member reformulates a supplied transfer generator and does not prove its gap.
66. Test trace-Dirichlet descent on a genuine regulated gauge carrier. Pass the volume-uniform strong-coupling Wilson Langevin Poincare bound both to the multiplicative cylinder pullback and through conditional expectation to the infimal interface trace, formulate the conditional reflection-Markov OS transport, prove the failure of naive semigroup compression without lumpability and the gauge-averaging kernel mismatch, and isolate the asymptotically free continuum trajectory as the decisive obstruction.
67. Test whether conditional expectation alone preserves the Markov property of an infimal trace. Construct a six-vertex graph counterexample whose positive Schur trace violates \(\check{\mathcal E}(|f|)\leq\check{\mathcal E}(f)\), proving that even the classical contraction property requires additional wall compatibility and that complete Dirichletness cannot be treated as formal closure.
68. Reverse the variance after the counterexample. For \(\nu=\pi_*\mu\) and the observable pullback \(Jf=f\circ\pi\), prove that \(\mathcal E^\leftarrow[f]=\mathcal E_W[Jf]\) is a closed Dirichlet form whenever densely defined, inherits the whole Poincare constant exactly, and is distinct from both the infimal quotient and the generally non-semigroup compression \(J^*P_tJ\). Use this safe branch as the primary finite-regulator carrier while retaining the infimal trace as a stronger conditional interpretation.
69. Lift the safe branch to standard forms. For a modular-invariant expected inclusion \(N\hookrightarrow M\), prove that its standard-form isometry preserves every amplified Markov interval, so restriction of a closed completely Dirichlet whole form is again closed and completely Dirichlet when densely defined. Prove that pullback equals the infimal quotient only when the expected subspace reduces the whole form or semigroup, and quarantine the arbitrary-quotient no-go from the exact Type-III pullback theorem.
70. Audit the metric transported by a nonlinear RG block. Derive \(G_B(y)=\mathbb E[DB\,M\,DB^*\mid B=y]\) for the cylinder mobility, require scale-uniform horizontal ellipticity against an independently chosen coarse metric, and use a product-measure counterexample to prove that a coarse edge alone cannot be inverted into fine coercivity. State the normalization-invariant physical target as the product of the cylinder edge and the best same-carrier OS-energy comparison coefficient, divided by the renormalized Yang--Mills scale.
71. Separate Euclidean attenuation from forgetting. For \(C_\ell=e^{-\ell H/(\hbar c)}\), prove injectivity and dense range at every finite \(\ell\), and prove \(\|C_\ell(I-P_0)\|=e^{-\ell\Delta_E/(\hbar c)}\). Locate genuine loss instead in an OS null quotient, conditional expectation, nonfaithful realization, or instrument rather than in the positive transfer semigroup itself.
72. Audit the fixed-spacing Wilson kinetic solder. Use full Peter--Weyl decomposition for \(SU(2)\) to prove that \(-\log P_T\geq\varepsilon D_{\mathrm H}\) fails on the untruncated carrier because \(-\log p_j\sim2j\log j\) while the group Casimir grows as \(j(j+1)\). Compute the sharp one-link bounded edge \(1-I_2(x)/I_1(x)>1/(x+1)\), tensorize without loss, and use closed spin-network support to prove the sharp gauge-invariant product edge \(1-[I_2(x)/I_1(x)]^{g(\Gamma)}\). Check that its tree-level temporal-continuum logarithmic rate equals the electric girth--Casimir floor. For the magnetic sandwich, derive the exact finite-volume Doob comparison and its action-only Harnack version, prove their exponential volume failure, and replace them by the local stationary-edge certificate \(I-P_T\geq\eta(I-e^{-\tau D_{\mathrm{cyl}}})\). Confine the unbounded flux identity to a genuine continuous-time Hamiltonian/common-form theorem.
73. Test whether faithful descent can manufacture the locally unitary clock. Prove that if \(E:M\to N\) is a faithful normal conditional expectation, \((\Phi_t)_{t\geq0}\) is a point-ultraweakly continuous normal UCP semigroup, and the expected maps \(E\Phi_t|_N\) are \(*\)-automorphisms, then \(\Phi_t(N)=N\) and \(N\) lies in the multiplicative domain of every \(\Phi_t\). With a faithful normal state invariant under \(\Phi_t\), the local GNS carrier is reducing; \(\varphi E=\varphi\) additionally gives \(E\Phi_t=\alpha_tE\) and the modular compatibility needed for the KMS standard-form version. If the corresponding GNS or symmetric \(L^2\) implementation is self-adjoint, the descended continuous same-parameter unitary group is trivial.
74. Complete the reciprocal-generator audit. Prove that two positive strongly commuting generators scaled with the same sign by one common modular group have a zero-or-gapless product on every common reducing sector, derive \(P_\pm=Me^{\pm\Theta}\) and \(H=M\cosh\Theta\) by joint Borel functional calculus on the kernel-free joint-positive carrier of an opposite-weight pair, and show that on a full positive-energy Poincare carrier one fixed physical null-pair floor is equivalent to both the Hamiltonian gap and the Casimir floor. Keep countable-dense angular tomography optional and full-carrier coverage load-bearing.
75. Localize the full two-slice angle without replacing physical transfer by a sampler. Identify Hirschfeld--Gebelein--Renyi maximal correlation with the centered conditional-transport norm, prove that \(1-\rho^2\) is the canonical whole-slice conditional-variance return and \(1/(1-\rho)\) is the optimal opposed-block variance-factorization constant, and decompose both endpoints into complete orthogonal innovations. Prove \(\rho\leq\|(\|D_i^XPD_j^Y\|)_{ij}\|_2\), together with its Schur and finite-range influence bounds. Construct the everywhere-positive reversible hidden-parity kernel in which every proper output block is independent of the whole input while \(\rho\to1\), and strengthen it by an injective Hilbert-positive perturbation with vanishing proper-block correlations, thereby ruling out isolated local marginals and one-slice heat-bath mixing as substitutes. Apply the theorem to the exact Perron-dressed Wilson pair law and make its boundary-uniform innovation matrix, or an independently proved same-carrier block form comparison, the next analytic target. Lift the two-slice projection geometry to modularly invariant Type-III expectations and Stinespring correspondences, while keeping BKM state tangents, GNS/KMS observable norms, OS energy, and Lorentzian unitarity as distinct carriers and arrows.
76. Close the finite-regulator gauge-filtration problem. Prove that conditional expectation onto every edge-subgraph sigma-algebra commutes with compact vertex gauge transformations for the actual invariant marginal, so it restricts to an orthogonal physical expectation. Order a spanning tree first, prove that every forest stage has only constant physical information, and let the chord stages resolve the complete centered carrier. Identify the same tower in maximal-tree coordinates as joint cycle holonomies modulo residual simultaneous conjugation, not as a tensor product of individual conjugacy classes. Calibrate the construction by showing that its Haar spin-network blocks diagonalize the pure product transfer and recover the exact girth contraction. For the interacting Perron law, isolate the remaining estimate as diagonal closure persistence plus summable off-diagonal vacuum influence, while preserving the induced-marginal, boundary-sector, noncommutative-algebra, Type-III, and continuum firewalls.
77. Construct the boundary-framed refinement rather than closing physical subregions independently. For an edge cut \(E=A\sqcup B\), impose Gauss law only at interior vertices and prove \(\mathcal H_{\mathrm{GI}}\cong(\mathcal H_A^{\mathrm{ext}}\widehat\otimes\mathcal H_B^{\mathrm{ext}})^{G^\partial}\cong\widehat\bigoplus_\lambda M_{A,\lambda}\widehat\otimes M_{B,\lambda^*}\). Prove that separately averaging the boundary keeps only the trivial sector and can erase a loop crossing the cut. Extend the carrier identity to faithful Wilson/Perron densities by the exact correlation-half-density intertwiner, while retaining its nonfactorizing action on conditional expectations. Make physical, dual-charge-glued innovation projections—not raw regional charged projections—the carrier of the next transfer estimate.
78. Separate the known strong-coupling gap from the continuum target. Record the finite-regulator Wilson transfer, strong-coupling exponential clustering, and Schor's isolated glueball pole as genuine physical precedents. Prove the scaling fact that a finite continuum energy forces the adjacent-slice contraction deficit to vanish linearly in temporal lattice spacing, while a fixed one-step deficit would make the physical energy diverge. State the unresolved theorem as a spatial-volume-uniform fixed-physical-slab contraction along \(\beta_{\mathrm{lat}}(a)\to\infty\), or the equivalent positive logarithmic rate of the complete glued innovation matrix, without substituting the Langevin sampler gap.
79. Vacuum-align the glued innovation resolution. For any orthogonal block family and pointed vacuum, prove that the vacuum complement is the orthogonal sum of all internally centered blocks and one canonical balance sector \(L^2(J,p)\ominus\mathbb C1\). Construct the compact-boundary-group isotypic and regional-innovation projections after diagonal Gauss gluing, transport them by the interacting correlation half-density, and prove with a two-link \(\mathbb Z_2\) receipt that omitting the balance block can erase the entire nonvacuum carrier. Make the complete matrix—including every balance row and column—the fixed-slab stopping condition. In Type III, locate the raw channel projections in an atomic higher relative commutant of a pointed boundary correspondence; do not promote the vacuum-aligned rank-one corrections to expectations or CP maps.
80. Separate the Fisher face of vacuum balance from its phase and rate faces. Identify the real balance carrier with the Fisher tangent of the block-weight law and prove the factor-four Born-amplitude relation and relative-entropy Hessian; show that imaginary balance directions leave every block weight fixed. Short the full transfer defect to balance after minimizing over internally centered extensions, derive the exact leave-and-return obstruction to a compressed semigroup, and require invariance plus cone positivity before calling label evolution Markov. Prove by the arbitrary-rate semigroup \(P_\Omega+e^{-\gamma\ell}(I-P_\Omega)\) that the static vacuum law and its entropy geometry cannot select the physical rate.
81. Replace the silently fixed carrier by a pointed transfer cocycle. For independently vacuum-aligned finite or countable carriers, prove exact composition of centered compressions and the entrywise lax law \(\mathscr B(RP)\leq\mathscr B(R)\mathscr B(P)\) for bounded block-norm matrices. Make the ordered complete-matrix product across a fixed physical slab the stopping object, allowing complementary stages of norm one to contract jointly. For contractive stages, telescope \(D_k=I-A_k^*A_k\) into an exact transported defect ledger and re-express joint contraction as a lower-frame inequality, thereby giving Hessian, flux, and boundary analyses a noncircular target when their comparison with \(D_k\) is independently proved. Derive the exact leave-and-return residue for an intermediate visible compression and separate deterministic memory from a stochastic approximation. Across changing Type-III states, use Haagerup standard-form unitaries for raw frame transport and bounded Araki amplitudes for pointing under domination; prove that nonunitary similarity generally destroys orthogonality, so vacuum alignment must be recomputed. Convert the product exponent into energy only after an independent OS-transfer intertwiner and into mass only after Poincare-Casimir reconstruction.
82. Construct the missing analysis map from a whole correspondence or bounded geometric response rather than from the unknown gap. For endpoint isometries $J_k^-,J_k^+$ into one joint carrier, derive $A_k=(J_k^+)^*J_k^-$ and the exact residue $L_k=(I-J_k^+(J_k^+)^*)J_k^-$ with $I-A_k^*A_k=L_k^*L_k$. For a separately supplied positive transfer and independently proved comparison $I-A_k\geq\eta_k(I-e^{-\tau_kG_k})$, use $I-A_k^2\geq I-A_k$ to define $L_k=\sqrt{\eta_k}(I-e^{-\tau_kG_k})^{1/2}$. Split the transported defect ledger into the response Gramian plus a positive unsoldered residual. Prove that a uniform fixed-slab floor for $\sum_kB_k^*L_k^*L_kB_k$ contracts the ordered product even when every $G_k$ has a nonvacuum kernel; distinguish finite-dimensional trivial-kernel coverage from an infinite-dimensional uniform lower frame. Route two-slice/Stinespring, edge-measure, conditional-expectation, flux, and closed-Hessian candidates into this theorem while keeping noninjective expectations separate from physical transfer and keeping formation, Euclidean attenuation, and Lorentzian clock unitarity as different arrows.
83. Complete the transfer cocycle isometrically rather than forcing it into a unitary group. For each contraction form the minimal defect column $V_Ax=Ax\oplus(I-A^*A)^{1/2}x$, prove it is onto exactly when $A$ is a coisometry, and hence prove that every nonidentity positive transfer has a proper non-surjective isometric completion. Cascade the columns across changing carriers, distinguish the full chronological archive from the minimal defect space of the endpoint product, split every defect into geometric response plus positive residual, and use Douglas factorization to type the solder as a contraction from the canonical transfer-defect carrier to the response carrier. For a fixed positive semigroup derive the continuous residue-density map $\sqrt{2K}e^{-sK}$ and prove that a finite-depth residue floor is equivalent to a lower generator edge. Keep ambient output concatenation separate from an unconstructed product system or $E_0$-semigroup, keep the stationary construction separate from an unproved changing-carrier continuum limit, and keep candidate record amplitude separate from entropy, actual outcomes, fundamental irreversibility, and Lorentzian clock unitarity.
84. Make pointed correspondence composition and chronological prediction residue exact without conflating them. For normal CP maps, identify the GNS correspondence of the composite with the cyclic subcorrespondence of the fused stage carrier, take the adjoint coisometry, and isolate its orthogonal intermediate-route residue; prove associativity and the subproduct-system rather than automatic product-system law. Show that the canonical endpoint lift has zero fusion residue and that a local endpoint unitary extends block-diagonally and intertwines with the descent instead of being caused by it. Separately realize the physical defect $I-P^{2n}$ as the pullback of an orthogonal reverse-martingale innovation archive on the stationary path carrier, retain $P^*$ without reversibility and the period-two obstruction without Hilbert positivity, and distinguish outer-vacuum fixed-slab preparation from the balanced periodic two-cut law.
85. Construct the history-sensitive fusion solder by inserting an arbitrary physical middle-slice observable into a stationary $2n$-step path and projecting away its conditional expectation given both endpoints. Identify the resulting range with the scalarized centered bridge residue, prove that its Gramian is average conditional bridge variance, and use nested projection order to obtain the exact upper bounds by $I-(P^*)^nP^n$ and $I-P^n(P^*)^n$, hence by $I-P^{2n}$ reversibly. Identify the same map as the score of an endpoint-preserving bridge tilt and retain the factor-four distinction between Fisher score and GNS half-density tangent. Prove fiberwise Doob/Perron cancellation while retaining the Perron-weighted endpoint base law. Make a regulator-uniform bridge lower frame at fixed physical half-slab--not nonzero residue rank, full support, or one-step positivity--the remaining analytic theorem.
86. Identify the bridge Gramian as the defect of the canonical middle--boundary-pair data-augmentation chain $S_n=K_n^*K_n$. Prove its exchangeable bridge-resampling edge law, exact fixed-space criterion, physical order $P^{2n}\leq S_n$, product maximal-correlation tensorization, gauge-restriction monotonicity, and complete-innovation bound. Compose its edge measure with an independently gapped same-carrier response semigroup to isolate the missing temporal solder. Make either that edge comparison or a subunit complete bridge-innovation matrix the next Wilson estimate, and retain the adjacent-slice scaling, Perron norm, boundary-charge, balance-sector, and continuum firewalls.
87. Calibrate the bridge floor exactly in the stationary Gaussian sector. For covariance $e^{-\omega|s-t|}$ and endpoint half-separation $\ell$, prove by Gaussian regression and Hermite chaos that the full centered bridge Gramian has sharp floor $\tanh(\omega\ell)$, not merely its linear-coordinate restriction. Tensorize the result over free-field modes to obtain $\kappa_{\rm field}=\tanh(\ell\operatorname*{ess,inf}\Omega)$, distinguish the exact Gaussian inverse $\omega=\ell^{-1}\operatorname{artanh}\kappa$ from the weaker universal logarithmic bridge bound, and type the dimensional conversion as $\Delta_E=\hbar c\omega$ or $M=\hbar\omega/c$ without pretending to derive the yardstick.
88. Reverse the local-mixing strategy onto the whole spacetime law. Partition a finite Euclidean cylinder into initial boundary, open interior, and final boundary; prove that any three-block variance factorization leaves only the midpoint bridge variance when tested on a middle-slice observable. Use weighted block entropy factorization and a three-macroblock Wasserstein--Dobrushin estimate as two established sufficient inputs. Identify the exact fixed-input chi-squared SDPI and relative quasi-factorization forms, and prove by reversible high-girth expander families whose Markov operators are positive semidefinite on \(L^2\) that neither a transfer gap nor qualitative absence of deterministic recovery implies a bridge floor. Then prove the boundary-adhesion no-go: a regulator-uniform disjoint full-carrier factorization is impossible in any continuum limit retaining a finite-energy direction. Replace it by overlapping boundary collars of fixed physical width, for which the midpoint compression is still exactly the bridge Gramian, and make the corresponding gauge-covariant, boundary-uniform Wilson factorization the next continuum target.
89. Make the Copernican unitarity claim functorial rather than causal. For an OS-bounded map commuting with Euclidean translations, prove that the induced map on OS null quotients intertwines the positive semigroups, their resolvents and spectral measures, and therefore the reconstructed Lorentzian clock groups. For a surjective map, prove the spectral-deletion identity \(E_{K_L}(B)=0\iff\operatorname{Ran}E_{K_W}(B)\subseteq\ker\widehat D\): a gapless whole carrier can have a gapped presentation precisely when its low spectral band is invisible. Prove separately that polynomial identities survive every surjective factor, so an idempotent expectation can descend to a unitary only as the identity; a positive Euclidean contraction can itself be unitary only when trivial. Use the direct-sum underdetermination witness to show that one fixed nonfaithful quotient is compatible with arbitrary local generators and gaps. Thus forgetting may coexist with local unitarity but neither manufactures nor selects it.
90. Reduce the collared bridge route to one two-block quasi-factorization. Combine the two end collars into a disconnected block, use conditioning monotonicity to pass its middle defect to the two-endpoint bridge, and retype Wilson plaquettes as bounded range-one interactions of outgoing-link cell spins. Test Cesi's finite-range complete-analyticity theorem against transverse-volume and finite-mass continuum scaling; isolate its surface-cardinality loss and replace it by a regulator-uniform Hilbertian surface-response angle as the next action-derived target. Keep the whole probability object, its noninvertible conditional expectations, Euclidean attenuation, and reconstructed local unitary clock as four different types.

| Poincare Casimir | for a strongly continuous unitary representation of the connected Poincare group, let \(E_{(H/c,\mathbf P)}\) be the translation spectral measure and \(P_0:=E_{(H/c,\mathbf P)}(\{0\})\); if its support lies in \(\overline V_+\), then \(H\geq\Delta(1-P_0)\) iff \(H^2-c^2|\mathbf P|^2\geq\Delta^2(1-P_0)\); \(P_0\) is rank one only under vacuum uniqueness, and on an irreducible positive-energy sector \(P^2/c^2=m^2 1\) | mass labels a relativistic orbit or sector while momentum varies within it; after Poincare reconstruction the Hamiltonian gap and Casimir floor are equivalent | first construct the Poincare-covariant physical carrier and exclude every nonvacuum null or arbitrarily light sector; a generic finite lattice has no exact Casimir, so prove its Hamiltonian bound first |

| Observable-pullback Dirichlet form | for \(\nu=\pi_*\mu\), the isometry \(Jf=f\circ\pi\) is multiplicative and commutes with every normal contraction, so the restriction \(\mathcal E_W[Jf]\) is closed and Markovian when densely defined and inherits the whole Poincare edge | gives a safe local process forced by the whole response and marginal state; clarifies that states descend while observables pull back | prove full neutral-carrier coverage, complete Dirichletness on the represented observable algebra, scale-normalized RG stability, OS identification, and physical-energy comparison |
| Standard-form pullback | a modular-invariant inclusion with preserving expectation induces matrix-level standard-form interval maps; restriction of a closed completely Dirichlet form is closed and completely Dirichlet on a dense restricted domain | proves that the safe observable-pullback branch extends to expected Type-III inclusions; under form reduction it also equals the infimal quotient | construct the physical expected inclusion and whole form; establish domain density, modular and net covariance, vacuum-kernel compatibility, continuum control, and the physical-energy solder |
| Two-expectation angle process | for tracial expectations, \(E_PE_QE_P\) is a GNS-symmetric UCP map and its Poisson generator \(I-pqp\) has sharp reduced edge \(1-c_F^2\) | relative position of two information-losing maps canonically supplies a dimensionless quantum Markov process on their disagreement carrier | its kernel is the entire common range; construct a vacuum-complete physical analysis map, fix the rate normalization, and prove one combined regulator-uniform carrier/angle/energy bound |
| Paired-wall short | shorting \(\alpha(I-P)+\beta(I-Q)\) to \(P\mathcal H\) gives \(\alpha\beta(I-C)(\alpha I+\beta C)^{-1}\), \(C=PQP\), with reduced edge \(\alpha\beta(1-c_F^2)/(\alpha+\beta c_F^2)\) | a second wall can leave a positive tangential response after every hidden lift is relaxed; one wall alone vanishes on its retained carrier | positivity does not imply a Markov or Lindblad generator; the OS tanh member is circular if the transfer Hamiltonian supplied the walls, and the physical pair and normalization remain to be derived |
| Gauge Dirichlet descent | the volume-uniform strong-coupling Wilson Langevin Poincare inequality passes both to the closed Markov cylinder form and to the infimal interface trace with the same \(K_S\) | first noncircular regulated gauge-theory example of a whole form forcing a positive interface edge; the two arrows separate safe observable testing from least-cost fibre elimination | prove full neutral-carrier coverage, complete observable-algebra implementation, same-core energy comparison, and a scale-normalized uniform estimate along the asymptotically free continuum trajectory; Langevin time is not clock time |

| no-interior reconstruction | an operational exterior-algebra map defines equivalence classes of pre-observable states without requiring interior-localized objects in its target | a black hole can first be posed as an exterior equivalence class labelled by recovered charges; any sector claim requires a GNS representation and a declared factoriality, quasi-equivalence, disjointness, or central criterion | reconstruct all exterior observables, charges, horizon thermodynamics, causal response, ringdown, and information accounting without importing the GR interior |
| Gauge descent | finite lattice gauge theory has a gauge-invariant physical carrier and Kogut--Susskind Hamiltonian | the gap need not be a gluon propagator mass | gauge reduction alone does not imply coercivity |
| Ground-state transform | the regulated Hamiltonian becomes a vacuum-weighted flux Dirichlet form | exact finite-regulator Poincare-inequality/Dirichlet-form reformulation | vacuum measure and lower constant must be controlled uniformly |
| Finite-spacing transfer solder | for the vacuum-normalized Wilson transfer, \(H_T-E_0=-(\hbar c/a_\tau)\log P_T\), but no positive full-carrier constant can satisfy \(-\log P_T\geq\varepsilon D_{\mathrm H}\) at fixed spacing because \(-\log p_j\sim2j\log j=o(j(j+1))\); nevertheless the pure \(SU(2)\) Wilson defect has sharp one-link edge \(1-I_2(x)/I_1(x)>1/(x+1)\), sharp gauge-invariant product edge \(1-[I_2(x)/I_1(x)]^{g(\Gamma)}\), and logarithmic rate \((\hbar c/a_\tau)g(\Gamma)\log(I_1/I_2)\) | with \(x=4a_s/(g_0^2a_\tau)\), that pure-kinetic rate tends to the Kogut--Susskind electric girth--Casimir floor; for the full magnetic sandwich, direct Doob-form comparison gives an exact finite-volume edge multiplied by \((a_-/a_+)^2(\rho_-/\rho_+)\), while the action-only Harnack version deteriorates exponentially with volume | replace the global density ratio by a local stationary two-slice certificate \(I-P_T\geq\eta(I-e^{-\tau D_{\mathrm{cyl}}})\) derived independently of the unknown spectrum; with \(q=\eta(1-e^{-\tau\lambda_{\mathrm{cyl}}})\), keep \((\hbar c/a_\tau)\log((1-q)^{-1})/\Lambda_{\mathrm{YM}}\) uniformly positive through volume and continuum removal; the unbounded flux identity belongs only to the temporal-continuum Hamiltonian limit |
| Haar flux frame | Peter--Weyl diagonalizes the product Laplacian; gauge invariants on a connected graph containing a cycle decompose into closed spin networks | the sharp pure-gauge constant is $g(\Gamma)\lambda_G$, which does not deteriorate with volume and is constant on fixed-girth lattice families | this is kinematic closure, not the interacting vacuum, continuum dynamics, or three-dimensional knotting |

| RG | asymptotic freedom supplies a scheme-labelled transmutation scale | best current dimensional yardstick for pure four-dimensional Yang--Mills | RG scale alone does not prove an infrared spectral gap |
| RG crossover | strong-coupling functional inequalities prove a useful finite-regulator precedent, while the continuum trajectory approaches weak bare coupling | block at an independently fixed physical scale and ask for uniform conditional coercivity after ultraviolet integration | each block contains diverging microscopic degrees of freedom as \(a\to0\); proving the block comparison is the multiscale problem, not a shortcut around it |
| Two-scale RG transfer | entropy splits exactly into coarse entropy plus conditional fiber entropy; linear and nonlinear two-scale LSI theorems combine fiber and coarse constants with a macro--micro coupling penalty | a theorem-shaped recursion for transporting coercivity through blocking and a precise location for the residue | adapt from smooth Euclidean submersions to stratified gauge quotients, control the full polymer action, prove summable loss through infinitely many UV steps, and compare auxiliary mixing with the OS Hamiltonian |
| Block-induced mobility | pulling a coarse observable through \(B\) gives \(G_B(y)=\mathbb E[DB\,M\,DB^*\mid B=y]\); a fixed coarse edge can coexist with a vanishing fine fiber edge | the block map itself transports a metric and preserves Markovianity, but a physically meaningful cross-scale comparison requires horizontal ellipticity and macro--micro control | derive the gauge-horizontal tensor for the actual RG maps, prove scale-uniform bounds, control every UV conditional fiber, and establish a normalization-invariant OS-energy comparison |
| Mosco convergence | generalized form convergence handles changing Hilbert spaces | a route for passing uniform coercivity to a continuum form | vacuum projections, nontriviality, locality, and reconstruction must also converge |
| local covariance/recovery | AQFT is functorial in regions/spacetimes; scaling algebras pose intrinsic short-distance limits | typed comparison and recovery contracts | natural state/observable/dynamics/form maps and quantitative remainders are absent |

### Fixed-direction Poincare theorem

Let \(U\) be a strongly continuous unitary representation of the connected Poincare group, let \(E_{(H/c,\mathbf P)}\) be the joint spectral measure of its translation generators, and assume

$$
\operatorname{supp}E_{(H/c,\mathbf P)}\subseteq\overline V_+.
$$

Put

$$
P_0:=E_{(H/c,\mathbf P)}(\{0\}),
\qquad
\mathcal C:=H^2-c^2|\mathbf P|^2,
$$

and, for a fixed unit vector \(\mathbf n\),

$$
P_\pm(\mathbf n):=H\pm c\,\mathbf P\!\cdot\!\mathbf n,
\qquad
Q_{\mathbf n}:=P_+(\mathbf n)P_-(\mathbf n).
$$

All products are the nonnegative self-adjoint operators defined by the joint Borel functional calculus. The zero-translation projection \(P_0\) reduces them; it is rank one only under the additional unique-vacuum hypothesis. For every \(\Delta>0\) and every one fixed \(\mathbf n\),

$$
\boxed{
Q_{\mathbf n}\geq\Delta^2(I-P_0)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
H\geq\Delta(I-P_0)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\mathcal C\geq\Delta^2(I-P_0).}
$$

These are spectral operator inequalities, equivalently closed quadratic-form inequalities on their natural form domains. The first implication follows from \(H\geq Q_{\mathbf n}^{1/2}\); the Hamiltonian-to-Casimir implication uses Lorentz invariance of the joint spectral support; and the reverse directional implication uses

$$
Q_{\mathbf n}
=
\mathcal C+c^2\bigl(|\mathbf P|^2-(\mathbf P\!\cdot\!\mathbf n)^2\bigr).
$$

For any countable dense \(D\subset S^2\), the common joint spectral representation also gives the pointwise identity

$$
\mathcal C(p)=\inf_{\mathbf n\in D}Q_{\mathbf n}(p).
$$

Thus the dense angular family supplies direct pointwise Casimir tomography. It is stronger than, and is not required for, the gap implication once one fixed physical null pair has a full-carrier floor after Poincare reconstruction.

| pointed CP fusion residue | for normal CP maps $\Phi:A\to B$ and $\Psi:B\to C$, Paschke GNS gives a canonical isometry $E_{\Psi\Phi}\hookrightarrow E_\Phi\bar\otimes_BE_\Psi$ onto the tensor-point cyclic subcorrespondence; self-duality makes its adjoint a coisometry and its orthogonal kernel the fusion residue; the inclusions associate and pointwise semigroup fibers form a subproduct system | supplies an exact correspondence-level endpoint descent and identifies what it omits as intermediate-factorization or bridge directions; endpoint unitaries lift to the retained corner and can extend across the residue, so coexistence is expressed by an intertwiner rather than causal production | the CP maps are inputs; neither carrier is automatically physical or local, the coisometry is noninvertible only when the residue is nonzero, and nonzero residue rank persists while a two-state generator edge tends to zero |
| bridge-score fusion solder | on a stationary $2n$-step path, $L_n^{\rm br}=(I-\mathsf E_{0,2n})J_n$ maps every middle-slice $L^2$ observable into the centered bridge fusion residue; $B_n^{\rm br}=L_n^*L_n$ is average conditional bridge variance and obeys $0\leq B_n^{\rm br}\leq I-(P^*)^nP^n$ and $B_n^{\rm br}\leq I-P^n(P^*)^n$, hence $B_n^{\rm br}\leq I-P^{2n}$ reversibly; it is also the Fisher Gramian of endpoint-preserving middle tilts, while the GNS half-density Gramian is $B_n^{\rm br}/4$ | supplies the missing complete, history-sensitive slice-to-fusion analysis and its exact upper solder without inserting the desired spectrum; Doob/Perron factors cancel in each bridge fiber, exposing a raw-kernel Wilson conditional law | prove $B_{n(a)}^{\rm br}\geq\kappa_*Q$ uniformly at fixed physical half-slab on the complete gauge-invariant carrier; fiberwise cancellation does not remove the Perron-weighted endpoint norm, full support gives no uniform constant, and the implication to a transfer gap is sufficient rather than equivalent |
| bridge data-augmentation solder | the middle-to-boundary conditional transport $K_n$ gives a positive reversible auxiliary chain $S_n=K_n^*K_n$ with $B_n^{\rm br}=I-S_n$ and exchangeable edge law $\int\beta_z\otimes\beta_z\,\mathrm d\mathsf J(z)$; $P^{2n}\leq S_n$ reversibly, maximal correlation tensorizes by a maximum on product link bridges, gauge restriction preserves a raw floor, and complete middle/boundary innovations give $I-S_n\geq(1-\|C\|^2)Q$ | turns the lower-frame problem into a canonical two-component Gibbs/data-augmentation Poincare problem already soldered to physical transfer; avoids the exponential volume loss of a product density minorization and supplies an exact edge-measure route through $I-S_n\geq\eta(I-e^{-\tau D})$ | prove either the bridge-resampling edge comparison $\mathsf M_n^\circ\geq\eta\mathsf J_{e^{-\tau D}}^\circ$ with an independently derived auxiliary edge, or $\|C_{a,L,\mathsf s}^{\rm br}(\ell_*)\|\leq q_*<1$, uniformly for the actual Perron law; one-slice Dobrushin control alone cannot supply the temporal coefficient |
| Gaussian bridge-gap calibration | for a stationary Gaussian mode with covariance $e^{-\omega|s-t|}$, Gaussian regression and Hermite chaos give the sharp full-carrier bridge floor $\kappa(\ell)=\tanh(\omega\ell)$; product maximal-correlation tensorization gives $\kappa_{\rm field}=\tanh(\ell\operatorname*{ess\,inf}\Omega)$ | proves that dimensionless two-ended nonrecoverability and the inverse Compton rate are equivalent data in the free Gaussian sector; supplies the exact conversion $\Delta_E=(\hbar c/\ell)\operatorname{artanh}\kappa$ for the optimal Gaussian floor | the artanh law is not a generic interacting functional calculus, and neither $\ell$, $c$, nor $\hbar$ is derived; a certified suboptimal floor gives only a lower bound, while Yang--Mills still needs the uniform Wilson bridge theorem |
| collared whole-slab bridge factorization | a whole-law block inequality gives the bridge floor when tested on midpoint observables; however, the disjoint full-carrier constant obeys $C_{\rm BF}^{\rm dis}\geq(1-\|PQ\|^2)^{-1}$ and must diverge under temporal refinement if a finite-energy direction survives; overlapping boundary collars remove this elementary adhesion mode; more economically, a two-block cover consisting of the disconnected end collars and the open interior already gives the bridge floor | Cesi's theorem accepts finite-range many-body compact-spin interactions, and the outgoing-link cell encoding makes each Wilson plaquette a range-one three-cell factor, closing the finite-regulator hyperedge-typing gap | prove complete analyticity or a weaker $L^2$ response criterion uniformly on the Wilson continuum trajectory; Cesi's sufficient defect is proportional to transverse boundary area, so replace that proof loss by a tensorizing surface-response angle while retaining boundary charges, Perron preparation, and every physical sector |
| reverse-prediction residue archive | on a stationary path, the decreasing future filtration gives $\mathsf E_kJ_0f=J_k(P^*)^kf$; under reversibility its orthogonal increments satisfy $J_0^*(\mathsf E_k-\mathsf E_{k+1})J_0=P^k(I-P^2)P^k$ and sum to $I-P^{2n}$; a fixed-slab innovation floor $\kappa$ is exactly $1-\|P^n\|^2$ and gives $\Delta_E\geq-(\hbar c/2\ell)\log(1-\kappa)$ after OS identification | realizes every abstract chronological defect coordinate as a conditional-prediction residue in one path carrier without asserting stochastic ontology; the finite Wilson Doob path follows from outer vacuum preparation, and the exact stopping condition is uniform finite-depth innovation capture on the complete physical complement | it repackages rather than proves contraction; retain $P^*$ without reversibility, exclude period-two $-1$ modes by Hilbert positivity, do not replace the fixed-inner-slab law by the balanced periodic two-cut law, and prove the innovation floor uniformly through continuum and Poincare reconstruction |

## Derived no-go: ordinary local expectation shells

Let $\mathcal N\subseteq\mathcal M$ be nested local observable algebras on the vacuum Hilbert space. Assume $\Omega$ is cyclic for $\mathcal N$ and separating for $\mathcal M$. If $E:\mathcal M\to\mathcal N$ is a vacuum-preserving conditional expectation, then for $n\in\mathcal N$ and $a\in\mathcal M$,

$$
\langle n\Omega,(a-Ea)\Omega\rangle
=
\omega(n^*a)-\omega(n^*Ea)
=0.
$$

Because $\mathcal N\Omega$ is dense, $(a-Ea)\Omega=0$; because $\Omega$ is separating for $\mathcal M$, $a=Ea$. Hence $\mathcal N=\mathcal M$. Under the usual Reeh--Schlieder hypotheses, a nontrivial vacuum-preserving expectation tower cannot be the ordinary nested region net. Regulator/RG block algebras, altered carriers or states, or controlled inequalities are required.

This is stronger than the generic warning that modular invariance is restrictive and is a decisive routing constraint for the proposed framework.

## Derived type separation: factivity is not spectral coercivity

For a finite commutative context $\mathcal C\cong C(X)$, a state restricts to a probability law on $X$, while a fact is a character $\chi_x(f)=f(x)$. A completely positive instrument fixes probabilities and conditional states but, absent a further actuality rule, still does not supply the obtained $x$. A record is a further directed extension preserving the chosen character. These are separate from a coarse map $E$, whose defect $1-E$ can measure lost distinctions, and separate again from the physical energy form $h$.

The proposed common process therefore has four typed arrows:

$$
\text{descent/gluing}
\ ;\quad
\text{nonfaithful forgetting}
\ ;\quad
\text{factive pointing and record}
\ ;\quad
\text{energy coercivity}.
$$

Only the last arrow defines a spectral gap. The phrase “the whole is not unitary so that the local can be unitary” is therefore defensible only as a typed carrier statement, not as a causal theorem: unitarity is relative to a positive form and an identity, and a unitary in the retained corner has identity \(e\) even when its whole-carrier representative is noninvertible relative to \(I\). [[algebra/os-descent-naturality-and-clock-no-go|The polynomial factor theorem]] sharpens this: an idempotent expectation cannot literally descend to a nonidentity unitary, while an OS-natural nonfaithful map can intertwine a separately reconstructed clock group. Under faithful expected descent, exact local automorphy instead identifies an already invariant noiseless sector; forgetting does not manufacture it. The useful new hypothesis is that the earlier wall operations construct and canonically normalize the same physical distinction family whose integrated defect form satisfies the lower-frame estimate. This remains a construction target, not a consequence of measurement theory.

## Primary sources used

### Yang--Mills target and regulated carrier

- Arthur Jaffe and Edward Witten, “Quantum Yang--Mills Theory,” official [Clay PDF](https://www.claymath.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/06/yangmills.pdf). The formal statement is on p. 5; pp. 10--11 discuss regulator and volume control and the possible need for a nonclassical change of variables or duality.
- Clay Mathematics Institute, [“Yang--Mills & the Mass Gap”](https://www.claymath.org/millennium/yang-mills-the-maths-gap/). The current official page continues to label the problem unsolved and says no proof is known.
- Mir Faizal and Arshid Shabir, [[library/reflection-positive-construction-of-four-dimensional-su-n-yang-mills-theory-with-mass-gap-and-confinement/inq|“Reflection-Positive Construction of a Four-Dimensional SU(N) Yang--Mills Theory with Mass Gap and Confinement,”]] *Fortschritte der Physik* **74** (2026), e70097, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1002/prop.70097), [arXiv:2606.19362v1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19362v1). This is recorded as a published claimed construction and a useful theorem architecture, not an accepted solution; the audit above identifies unresolved quantitative steps in its continuum-gap transport.
- Kenneth G. Wilson, [[library/confinement-of-quarks/inq|“Confinement of Quarks,”]] *Physical Review D* **10** (1974), 2445--2459, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevD.10.2445). Introduces the exact gauge-invariant lattice regulator and plaquette action recovered by the exceptional normal on its color pullback.
- John Kogut and Leonard Susskind, [[library/hamiltonian-formulation-of-wilsons-lattice-gauge-theories/inq|“Hamiltonian Formulation of Wilson's Lattice Gauge Theories,”]] *Physical Review D* **11** (1975), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevD.11.395). Provides the finite lattice Hamiltonian framework consumed by the exact local ground-state-transform note.
- Martin Luscher, [[library/construction-of-a-selfadjoint-strictly-positive-transfer-matrix-for-euclidean-lattice-gauge-theories/inq|“Construction of a Selfadjoint, Strictly Positive Transfer Matrix for Euclidean Lattice Gauge Theories,”]] *Communications in Mathematical Physics* **54** (1977), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01614090), [open DESY record](https://bib-pubdb1.desy.de/record/396349/files). Constructs the finite-regulator positive transfer operator and its Hamiltonian interpretation.
- Michael Creutz, [[library/gauge-fixing-the-transfer-matrix-and-confinement-on-a-lattice/inq|“Gauge Fixing, the Transfer Matrix, and Confinement on a Lattice,”]] *Physical Review D* **15** (1977), 1128--1136, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevD.15.1128). Extracts the Kogut--Susskind Hamiltonian from the Wilson transfer construction in the limit of vanishing timelike lattice spacing; it does not identify the two generators at fixed spacing.
- Frithjof Karsch, [[library/su-n-gauge-theory-couplings-on-asymmetric-lattices/inq|“SU(N) Gauge Theory Couplings on Asymmetric Lattices,”]] *Nuclear Physics B* **205** (1982), 285--300, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1016/0550-3213(82)90390-X), [open record](https://noah.nrw/ubbihs/content/titleinfo/5114815). Shows that the temporal Hamiltonian limit requires independently renormalized spatial and temporal couplings on an anisotropic lattice.
- Timothy R. Klassen, [[library/the-anisotropic-wilson-gauge-action/inq|“The Anisotropic Wilson Gauge Action,”]] *Nuclear Physics B* **533** (1998), 557--575, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1016/S0550-3213(98)00510-0), [arXiv:hep-lat/9803010](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-lat/9803010). Separates bare from renormalized anisotropy and supplies the standard anisotropic Wilson-coupling convention used in the temporal-rate audit.
- R. P. Soni, [[library/on-an-inequality-for-modified-bessel-functions/inq|“On an Inequality for Modified Bessel Functions,”]] *Journal of Mathematics and Physics* **44** (1965), 406--407, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1002/sapm1965441406). Gives the strict order monotonicity that locates the first nontrivial Wilson-convolution eigenvalue.
- Ingemar Näsell, [[library/inequalities-for-modified-bessel-functions/inq|“Inequalities for Modified Bessel Functions,”]] *Mathematics of Computation* **28** (1974), 253--256, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1090/S0025-5718-1974-0333288-9). Gives the explicit ratio bound \(I_2(x)/I_1(x)<x/(x+1)\).
- Pietro Menotti and Enrico Onofri, [[library/the-action-of-su-n-lattice-gauge-theory-in-terms-of-the-heat-kernel-on-the-group-manifold/inq|“The Action of SU(N) Lattice Gauge Theory in Terms of the Heat Kernel on the Group Manifold,”]] *Nuclear Physics B* **190** (1981), 288--300, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1016/0550-3213(81)90560-5), [CERN record](https://cds.cern.ch/record/134274). The heat-kernel action has exact Casimir-exponential representation weights and therefore supplies a cleaner finite-step kinetic comparison model, not by itself a four-dimensional continuum gap.
- Konrad Osterwalder and Erhard Seiler, “Gauge Field Theories on a Lattice,” *Annals of Physics* **110** (1978), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1016/0003-4916(78)90039-8). Verifies physical positivity and obtains a positive self-adjoint transfer matrix for the regulated lattice theory.
- Martin Luscher, Rajamani Narayanan, Peter Weisz, and Ulli Wolff, “The Schrodinger Functional: A Renormalizable Probe for Non-Abelian Gauge Theories,” *Nuclear Physics B* **384** (1992), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1016/0550-3213(92)90466-O), [arXiv:hep-lat/9207009](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-lat/9207009). Supplies the temporal-boundary amplitude and its perturbative continuum renormalization framework.
- David Gross and Frank Wilczek, “Ultraviolet Behavior of Non-Abelian Gauge Theories,” *Physical Review Letters* **30** (1973), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevLett.30.1343); H. David Politzer, “Reliable Perturbative Results for Strong Interactions?” *Physical Review Letters* **30** (1973), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevLett.30.1346). Establish asymptotic freedom; they do not establish a mass gap.
- Isadore M. Singer, “Some Remarks on the Gribov Ambiguity,” *Communications in Mathematical Physics* **60** (1978), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01609471), [primary scan](https://projecteuclid.org/journals/communications-in-mathematical-physics/volume-60/issue-1/Some-remarks-on-the-Gribov-ambiguity/cmp/1103904019.pdf). Proves a no-global-section obstruction for the compact-base nonabelian configuration classes treated there; it licenses an atlas warning, not a gap mechanism or a claim that local gauges never exist.
- Margarita Garcia Perez, Antonio Gonzalez-Arroyo, and Masanori Okawa, [[library/spatial-volume-dependence-for-2-1-dimensional-su-n-yang-mills-theory/inq|“Spatial Volume Dependence for 2+1 Dimensional SU(N) Yang--Mills Theory,”]] *Journal of High Energy Physics* **09** (2013) 003, [arXiv:1307.5254](https://arxiv.org/abs/1307.5254). Derives the color-momentum and electric-flux organization on an irreducibly twisted spatial torus; its neutral opposite-flux threshold is finite-volume and scales as \(1/L\).
- Margarita Garcia Perez, Antonio Gonzalez-Arroyo, Masanori Koren, and Masanori Okawa, [[library/the-spectrum-of-2-1-dimensional-yang-mills-theory-on-a-twisted-spatial-torus/inq|“The Spectrum of 2+1 Dimensional Yang--Mills Theory on a Twisted Spatial Torus,”]] *Journal of High Energy Physics* **07** (2018) 169, [arXiv:1807.03481](https://arxiv.org/abs/1807.03481). Distinguishes zero-flux glueballs from torelons and neutral torelon pairs across the finite-volume crossover.
- Mithat Unsal and Laurence G. Yaffe, [[library/center-stabilized-yang-mills-theory-confinement-and-large-n-volume-independence/inq|“Center-Stabilized Yang--Mills Theory: Confinement and Large N Volume Independence,”]] *Physical Review D* **78** (2008) 065035, [arXiv:0803.0344](https://arxiv.org/abs/0803.0344). Shows in a controlled deformed small-circle regime that center holonomy leaves perturbatively massless Cartan photons and that monopole-instantons generate the positive neutral dual-photon Hessian; it does not prove deformation removal to ordinary finite-\(N\) \(\mathbb R^4\) Yang--Mills.

### Pointed CP composition and fusion

- William L. Paschke, [[library/inner-product-modules-over-b-star-algebras/inq|“Inner Product Modules over (B^*)-Algebras,”]] *Transactions of the American Mathematical Society* **182** (1973), 443--468, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1090/S0002-9947-1973-0355613-0). Theorem 5.2 constructs the Hilbert-module GNS representation of a completely positive map; the self-dual extension is the (W^*)-correspondence carrier used here.
- Orr Shalit and Baruch Solel, [[library/subproduct-systems/inq|“Subproduct Systems,”]] *Documenta Mathematica* **14** (2009), 801--868, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.4171/DM/290), [primary open article](https://ems.press/content/serial-article-files/26070). Theorem 3.2 proves that the pointwise GNS correspondences of a normal CP semigroup embed cyclically into their tensor products and that the adjoint coisometries form a subproduct system; it explicitly uses self-duality for adjointability.
- B. V. Rajarama Bhat and Michael Skeide, [[library/tensor-product-systems-of-hilbert-modules-and-dilations-of-completely-positive-semigroups/inq|“Tensor Product Systems of Hilbert Modules and Dilations of Completely Positive Semigroups,”]] *Infinite Dimensional Analysis, Quantum Probability and Related Topics* **3** (2000), 519--575, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1142/S0219025700000261). Their partition-directed construction supplies a product-system envelope and dilation; that completion must not be identified with the raw pointwise GNS fibers.

### Frames, forms, and continuum passage

- W. N. Anderson, Jr. and G. E. Trapp, [[library/shorted-operators-ii/inq|“Shorted Operators II,”]] *SIAM Journal on Applied Mathematics* **28** (1975), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1137/0128007). Prove existence of the largest positive operator below \(A\) whose range lies in a declared closed subspace.
- E. L. Pekarev, [[library/shorts-of-operators-and-some-extremal-problems/inq|“Shorts of Operators and Some Extremal Problems,”]] *Acta Scientiarum Mathematicarum* **56** (1992), [primary PDF](https://acta.bibl.u-szeged.hu/15339/1/math_056_fasc_001_002_147-163.pdf). Gives the variational formula expressing the short as the least whole quadratic cost over hidden extensions.
- Jorge Antezana, Gustavo Corach, and Demetrio Stojanoff, [[library/spectral-shorted-operators/inq|“Spectral Shorted Operators,”]] *Integral Equations and Operator Theory* **55** (2006), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00020-005-1382-4), [arXiv:math/0410573](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0410573). Record the ordinary short's intersection and iterated-shorting identities and exhibit its nontrivial continuity limits before studying a distinct spectral short.
- R. G. Douglas, [[library/on-majorization-factorization-and-range-inclusion-of-operators-on-hilbert-space/inq|“On Majorization, Factorization, and Range Inclusion of Operators on Hilbert Space,”]] *Proceedings of the American Mathematical Society* **17** (1966), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1090/S0002-9939-1966-0203464-1). Gives the range-inclusion/majorization equivalence behind the exact positive-floor criterion.
- S. T. Ali, J.-P. Antoine, and J.-P. Gazeau, “Continuous Frames in Hilbert Space,” *Annals of Physics* **222** (1993), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1006/aphy.1993.1016). Supplies the continuous-frame grammar and the meaning of a lower frame bound.
- Tim Netzer and Andreas Thom, [[library/kazhdans-property-t-via-semidefinite-optimization/inq|“Kazhdan's Property (T) via Semidefinite Optimization,”]] *Experimental Mathematics* **24** (2015), [arXiv:1411.2488](https://arxiv.org/abs/1411.2488). States the all-unitary-representations spectral characterization and gives an exact group-ring certificate for \(SL(3,\mathbb Z)\); applied contrapositively here, it distinguishes quantitative global rigidity from mere group discreteness.
- Sorin Popa and Stefaan Vaes, [[library/representation-theory-for-subfactors-lambda-lattices-and-c-star-tensor-categories/inq|“Representation Theory for Subfactors, Lambda-Lattices and C-Star Tensor Categories,”]] *Communications in Mathematical Physics* **340** (2015), [arXiv:1412.2732](https://arxiv.org/abs/1412.2732). Construct the universal categorical \(C^*\)-algebra of admissible representations and characterize property \((T)\) by its Kazhdan projection; the theorem supplies categorical rigidity, not a physical Hamiltonian.
- Stefaan Vaes and Matthias Valvekens, [[library/property-t-discrete-quantum-groups-and-subfactors-with-triangle-presentations/inq|“Property (T) Discrete Quantum Groups and Subfactors with Triangle Presentations,”]] *Advances in Mathematics* **345** (2019), [arXiv:1804.04006](https://arxiv.org/abs/1804.04006). Give the normalized finite-generator fusion average and identify isolation of its trivial universal eigenvalue with categorical property \((T)\).
- Corey Jones, [[library/quantum-g2-categories-have-property-t/inq|“Quantum \(G_2\) Categories Have Property (T),”]] *International Journal of Mathematics* **27** (2016), [arXiv:1504.08338](https://arxiv.org/abs/1504.08338). Proves property \((T)\) for the unitary type-1 \(U_q(\mathfrak g_2)\) categories at positive \(q\neq1\) using tube-algebra positivity, giving the exceptional categorical edge used here.
- Shamindra Kumar Ghosh and Corey Jones, [[library/annular-representation-theory-for-rigid-c-star-tensor-categories/inq|“Annular Representation Theory for Rigid \(C^*\)-Tensor Categories,”]] *Journal of Functional Analysis* **270** (2016), 1537--1584, [arXiv:1502.06543](https://arxiv.org/abs/1502.06543). Identify the tensor-unit annular corner with the fusion algebra and characterize Popa--Vaes admissible fusion representations as restrictions of full annular representations.
- Corey Jones and David Penneys, [[library/operator-algebras-in-rigid-c-star-tensor-categories/inq|“Operator Algebras in Rigid \(C^*\)-Tensor Categories,”]] *Communications in Mathematical Physics* **355** (2017), 1121--1188, [arXiv:1611.04620](https://arxiv.org/abs/1611.04620). Supply internal \(C^*\)- and \(W^*\)-algebra objects, categorical GNS, completely positive maps, and Stinespring dilation; this is the grammar for a measured fusion action, not its Yang--Mills construction.
- Luca Giorgetti and Wei Yuan, [[library/realization-of-rigid-c-star-tensor-categories-via-tomita-bimodules/inq|“Realization of Rigid \(C^*\)-Tensor Categories via Tomita Bimodules,”]] *Journal of Operator Theory* **81** (2019), 433--479, [arXiv:1712.09311](https://arxiv.org/abs/1712.09311). Realize arbitrary small rigid \(C^*\)-tensor categories with simple unit on Type II and Type III factors; existence on some synthetic factor does not identify the physical Yang--Mills net, state, or translations.
- Marcel Bischoff, Roberto Longo, Yasuyuki Kawahigashi, and Karl-Henning Rehren, [[library/tensor-categories-and-endomorphisms-of-von-neumann-algebras/inq|*Tensor Categories and Endomorphisms of von Neumann Algebras*]], SpringerBriefs in Mathematical Physics **3** (2015), [arXiv:1407.4793](https://arxiv.org/abs/1407.4793). Review Q-system reconstruction of finite-index extensions and distinguish the categorical dimension of the underlying dual canonical endomorphism from the square-root Q-system convention.
- Takashi Sano and Yasuo Watatani, [[library/angles-between-two-subfactors/inq|“Angles Between Two Subfactors,”]] *Journal of Operator Theory* **32** (1994), [primary article](https://jot.theta.ro/jot/archive/1994-032-002/1994-032-002-001.html). Define the subfactor angle through Jones-projection compressions; its largest cosine controls the reduced two-expectation edge.
- Vaughan F. R. Jones and Feng Xu, [[library/intersections-of-finite-families-of-finite-index-subfactors/inq|“Intersections of Finite Families of Finite Index Subfactors,”]] *International Journal of Mathematics* **15** (2004), [arXiv:math/0406331v2](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0406331v2). Relate finite common index to finite-dimensionality of the algebra generated by the expectation projections, with additional common-state and meet hypotheses in the general-factor extension.
- Keshab Chandra Bakshi and Satyajit Guin, [[library/relative-position-between-a-pair-of-spin-model-subfactors/inq|“Relative Position Between a Pair of Spin Model Subfactors,”]] *Journal of the Australian Mathematical Society* **119** (2025), [arXiv:2212.07197v3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2212.07197v3). Their index-\(2\) spin-model family keeps the common index \(4\) while its Friedrichs edge tends to zero, and can be represented with one fixed reducible common inclusion.
- Brian Lee Beers and Richard S. Millman, “The Spectra of the Laplace--Beltrami Operator on Compact, Semisimple Lie Groups,” *American Journal of Mathematics* **99** (1977), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.2307/2373866). Supplies a primary spectral-geometric source for the compact-group Laplacian/Casimir input after metric normalization.
