- John C. Baez, “Spin Network States in Gauge Theory,” *Advances in Mathematics* **117** (1996), [arXiv:gr-qc/9411007](https://arxiv.org/abs/gr-qc/9411007). Supplies the fixed-graph gauge-invariant spin-network basis used in the girth--Casimir deduction.
- Markus J. Pflaum, Gerd Rudolph, and Matthias Schmidt, [[library/deformation-quantization-and-homological-reduction-of-a-lattice-gauge-model/inq|“Deformation Quantization and Homological Reduction of a Lattice Gauge Model,”]] [arXiv:1912.12819v2](https://arxiv.org/abs/1912.12819v2) (2020), Section 2. They prove that the pointed vertex-gauge group acts freely, maximal-tree gauge identifies its quotient with \(G^{b_1(\Gamma)}\), and the residual root group acts by simultaneous conjugation. The cycle-filtration theorem uses only this kinematic reduction, not their later deformation-quantization claims.
- Christian W. Bauer, Irian D'Andrea, Marat Freytsis, and Dorota M. Grabowska, [[library/a-new-basis-for-hamiltonian-su-2-simulations/inq|“A New Basis for Hamiltonian \(SU(2)\) Simulations,”]] *Physical Review D* **109** (2024), 074501, [arXiv:2307.11829](https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.11829). Their maximal-tree construction exhibits one rooted loop per off-tree link and the nonlocal electric couplings induced by this gauge-fixed presentation; it supports the locality warning, not the gap estimate.
- William Donnelly, [[library/decomposition-of-entanglement-entropy-in-lattice-gauge-theory/inq|“Decomposition of Entanglement Entropy in Lattice Gauge Theory,”]] *Physical Review D* **85** (2012), 085004, [arXiv:1109.0036v2](https://arxiv.org/abs/1109.0036v2). His physical-to-open-region embedding and dual boundary-index contraction supply a primary lattice precedent for the boundary-framed gluing theorem; the theorem here is stated for a general shared-vertex edge cut and does not identify its carrier decomposition with an entropy formula.
- William Donnelly and Laurent Freidel, [[library/local-subsystems-in-gauge-theory-and-gravity/inq|“Local Subsystems in Gauge Theory and Gravity,”]] *Journal of High Energy Physics* **09** (2016) 102, [arXiv:1601.04744v2](https://arxiv.org/abs/1601.04744v2). Their boundary-frame construction and diagonal surface-singlet gluing give the direct primary precedent for treating dual cut charges as internal channels of one global gauge-invariant carrier; they do not supply transfer dynamics.
- Horacio Casini, Marina Huerta, and Jose Alejandro Rosabal, [[library/remarks-on-entanglement-entropy-for-gauge-fields/inq|“Remarks on Entanglement Entropy for Gauge Fields,”]] *Physical Review D* **89** (2014), 085012, [arXiv:1312.1183](https://arxiv.org/abs/1312.1183). They establish the regional-factorization obstruction, boundary centers, extended construction, and boundary maximal-tree ambiguity used as the localization firewall; they do not supply a transfer gap.
- Huzihiro Araki, [[library/some-properties-of-modular-conjugation-and-a-noncommutative-radon-nikodym-theorem-with-a-chain-rule/inq|“Some Properties of Modular Conjugation Operator of von Neumann Algebras and a Non-Commutative Radon--Nikodym Theorem with a Chain Rule,”]] *Pacific Journal of Mathematics* **50** (1974), 309--354, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.2140/pjm.1974.50.309), [primary article](https://msp.org/pjm/1974/50-2/pjm-v50-n2-p02-p.pdf). Theorem 7 supplies the natural-cone representative; Theorem 12 supplies, under domination, the bounded noncommutative Radon--Nikodym amplitude, its sharp norm characterization, and chain rule. The theorem does not provide a sharp-cut Type-III tensor factorization.
- Clement Delcamp, Bianca Dittrich, and Aldo Riello, [[library/fusion-basis-for-lattice-gauge-theory-and-loop-quantum-gravity/inq|“Fusion Basis for Lattice Gauge Theory and Loop Quantum Gravity,”]] *Journal of High Energy Physics* **02** (2017), 061, [arXiv:1607.08881](https://arxiv.org/abs/1607.08881). Separate the continuous-Haar lattice carrier from the discrete-topology presentation required to make the Lie-group flat/BF vacuum normalizable.
- Richard Holley and Daniel Stroock, “Logarithmic Sobolev Inequalities and Stochastic Ising Models,” *Journal of Statistical Physics* **46** (1987), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01011161). Supplies the bounded-perturbation comparison framework; the elementary Poincare density-ratio bound used here is its direct analogue.
- Liming Wu, “Poincare and Transportation Inequalities for Gibbs Measures under the Dobrushin Uniqueness Condition,” *The Annals of Probability* **34** (2006), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1214/009117906000000368), [arXiv:math/0611635](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0611635). Gives the sharp spectral-radius estimate assembling conditional Poincare bounds into a global inequality.
- Boguslaw Zegarlinski, “Dobrushin Uniqueness Theorem and Logarithmic Sobolev Inequalities,” *Journal of Functional Analysis* **105** (1992), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1016/0022-1236(92)90073-R). Gives an infinite-volume local-specification route from weak dependence to a logarithmic-Sobolev inequality.
- Alfred Renyi, [[library/on-measures-of-dependence/inq|“On Measures of Dependence,”]] *Acta Mathematica Academiae Scientiarum Hungaricae* **10** (1959), 441--451, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF02024507). Introduces maximal correlation and its optimization over square-integrable transformations; in a stationary pair law it is exactly the centered conditional-transport norm.
- Hans S. Witsenhausen, [[library/on-sequences-of-pairs-of-dependent-random-variables/inq|“On Sequences of Pairs of Dependent Random Variables,”]] *SIAM Journal on Applied Mathematics* **28** (1975), 100--113, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1137/0128010). Proves that maximal correlation of independent product pairs is the maximum of the component coefficients, explaining why the pure kinetic product transfer need not accumulate one loss factor per link.
- Jun S. Liu, Wing Hung Wong, and Augustine Kong, [[library/covariance-structure-of-the-gibbs-sampler-with-applications-to-the-comparisons-of-estimators-and-augmentation-schemes/inq|“Covariance Structure of the Gibbs Sampler with Applications to the Comparisons of Estimators and Augmentation Schemes,”]] *Biometrika* **81** (1994), 27--40, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1093/biomet/81.1.27), [accessible manuscript](https://www2.stat.duke.edu/~scs/Courses/Stat376/Papers/ConvergeRates/GibbsSampling/LiuWongKongBiometrika1994.pdf). Identify the marginal two-component data-augmentation rate with squared maximal correlation and show that grouping additional conditioning variables increases the relevant maximal correlation.
- Yaming Yu, [[library/on-the-maximal-correlation-coefficient/inq|“On the Maximal Correlation Coefficient,”]] *Statistics & Probability Letters* **78** (2008), 1072--1075, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.spl.2007.10.006). Gives a short proof of the Gebelein--Lancaster theorem that nonlinear transformations of a bivariate Gaussian pair cannot exceed its ordinary correlation; combined with Gaussian sufficiency and Hermite chaos, this makes the Gaussian bridge floor a full-$L^2$ result.
- Maxim Raginsky, [[library/strong-data-processing-inequalities-and-phi-sobolev-inequalities-for-discrete-channels/inq|“Strong Data Processing Inequalities and Phi-Sobolev Inequalities for Discrete Channels,”]] *IEEE Transactions on Information Theory* **62** (2016), 3355--3389, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1109/TIT.2016.2549542), [arXiv:1411.3575](https://arxiv.org/abs/1411.3575). Identifies fixed-input chi-squared contraction with squared maximal correlation and the channel Poincare energy with conditional residual variance.
- Alexander Lubotzky, Ralph Phillips, and Peter Sarnak, [[library/ramanujan-graphs/inq|“Ramanujan Graphs,”]] *Combinatorica* **8** (1988), 261--277, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF02126799), [manuscript](https://math1.math.huji.ac.il/~alexlub/PAPERS/ramanujan%20graphs/ramanujanGraphs.pdf). Supply the high-girth spectral expander families used to derive a positive reversible counterexample separating one-ended transfer contraction from two-ended bridge nonrecoverability; that bridge application is new here, not a claim of the source.
- Venkat Anantharam, Amin Gohari, Sudeep Kamath, and Chandra Nair, [[library/on-maximal-correlation-hypercontractivity-and-the-data-processing-inequality-studied-by-erkip-and-cover/inq|“On Maximal Correlation, Hypercontractivity, and the Data Processing Inequality Studied by Erkip and Cover,”]] [arXiv:1304.6133](https://arxiv.org/abs/1304.6133). Identify maximal-correlation squared as a local Hessian threshold and give a counterexample to its promotion to the optimal global mutual-information contraction coefficient.
- Yury Polyanskiy and Yihong Wu, [[library/strong-data-processing-inequalities-for-channels-and-bayesian-networks/inq|“Strong Data-Processing Inequalities for Channels and Bayesian Networks,”]] in *Convexity and Concentration* (2017), 211--249, [arXiv:1508.06025v4](https://arxiv.org/abs/1508.06025v4). Compare fixed-input chi-squared, maximal-correlation, and relative-entropy contraction coefficients; a fixed-stationary-law KL coefficient bounds the \(L^2\) coefficient but is not generally equal to it.
- Pietro Caputo, Georg Menz, and Prasad Tetali, [[library/approximate-tensorization-of-entropy-at-high-temperature/inq|“Approximate Tensorization of Entropy at High Temperature,”]] *Annales de la Faculte des sciences de Toulouse* **24** (2015), 691--716, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.5802/afst.1460). Give explicit weak-dependence conditions for volume-uniform entropy tensorization and heat-bath functional inequalities; the high-temperature sampler result is not a Wilson transfer comparison.
- Pietro Caputo and Daniel Parisi, [[library/block-factorization-of-the-relative-entropy-via-spatial-mixing/inq|“Block Factorization of the Relative Entropy via Spatial Mixing,”]] *Communications in Mathematical Physics* **388** (2021), 793--818, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00220-021-04237-1). Derive weighted block entropy factorization from strong spatial mixing for a class of lattice spin systems; linearization gives block variance factorization, but the hypotheses and heat-bath dynamics do not automatically cover the Wilson plaquette transfer.
- Filippo Cesi, [[library/quasi-factorization-of-the-entropy-and-logarithmic-sobolev-inequalities-for-gibbs-random-fields/inq|“Quasi-Factorization of the Entropy and Logarithmic Sobolev Inequalities for Gibbs Random Fields,”]] *Probability Theory and Related Fields* **120** (2001), 569--584, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/PL00008792). Proves two-sigma-algebra entropy quasi-factorization and applies it to finite-range summable interactions under complete analyticity without imposing a topology on the single-spin space; the proof-level dependence estimate carries a boundary-cardinality factor.
- Daniel W. Stroock and Boguslaw Zegarlinski, [[library/the-equivalence-of-the-logarithmic-sobolev-inequality-and-the-dobrushin-shlosman-mixing-condition/inq|“The Equivalence of the Logarithmic Sobolev Inequality and the Dobrushin--Shlosman Mixing Condition,”]] *Communications in Mathematical Physics* **144** (1992), 303--323, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF02101094). Prove the equivalence between a uniform logarithmic-Sobolev inequality and Dobrushin--Shlosman mixing for finite-range lattice gases with compact continuous spin space; applying it to Wilson cells still requires the boundary-uniform gauge specification hypotheses.
- Sourav Chatterjee, [[library/a-probabilistic-mechanism-for-quark-confinement/inq|“A Probabilistic Mechanism for Quark Confinement,”]] *Communications in Mathematical Physics* **385** (2021), 1007--1039, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00220-021-04086-y), [arXiv:2006.16229](https://arxiv.org/abs/2006.16229). Proves that arbitrary-boundary exponential correlation decay implies unbroken center symmetry and hence confinement for a nontrivial gauge-group center; the decay condition is a hypothesis, not a weak-coupling compact-\(SU(N)\) theorem.
- Arka Adhikari and Sky Cao, [[library/correlation-decay-for-finite-lattice-gauge-theories-at-weak-coupling/inq|“Correlation Decay for Finite Lattice Gauge Theories at Weak Coupling,”]] *The Annals of Probability* **53** (2025), 140--174, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1214/24-AOP1702), [arXiv:2202.10375](https://arxiv.org/abs/2202.10375). Prove weak-coupling correlation decay for gauge-invariant loop observables when the gauge group is finite, possibly non-Abelian; this is neither a continuous-\(SU(N)\) result nor a boundary-uniform entropy factorization.
- Ivan Bardet, Angela Capel, and Cambyse Rouze, [[library/approximate-tensorization-of-the-relative-entropy-for-noncommuting-conditional-expectations/inq|“Approximate Tensorization of the Relative Entropy for Noncommuting Conditional Expectations,”]] *Annales Henri Poincare* (2021), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00023-021-01088-3). Establish finite-dimensional von Neumann algebra quasi-factorization for intersecting noncommuting expectations and clustering-dependent constants; this is a noncommutative precedent rather than a Type-III or Yang--Mills theorem.
- Natalie Grunewald, Felix Otto, Cedric Villani, and Maria G. Westdickenberg, “A Two-Scale Approach to Logarithmic Sobolev Inequalities and the Hydrodynamic Limit,” *Annales de l'Institut Henri Poincare, Probabilites et Statistiques* **45** (2009), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1214/07-AIHP200), [journal PDF](https://www.numdam.org/item/AIHPB_2009__45_2_302_0.pdf). Gives the normalized linear two-scale LSI transfer and its explicit macro--micro coupling penalty.
- Herm Jan Brascamp and Elliott Lieb, “On Extensions of the Brunn--Minkowski and Prekopa--Leindler Theorems, Including Inequalities for Log Concave Functions, and with an Application to the Diffusion Equation,” *Journal of Functional Analysis* **22** (1976), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1016/0022-1236(76)90004-5). Supplies the inverse-Hessian covariance estimate and preservation of log-concavity under Euclidean marginalization.
- Thomas Louis, “Finding the Observed Information Matrix When Using the EM Algorithm,” *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society B* **44** (1982), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.2517-6161.1982.tb01203.x). Gives the statistical observed-information identity whose potential-theoretic form is conditional mean Hessian minus conditional score covariance.
- Bernard Helffer and Johannes Sjostrand, “On the Correlation for Kac-Like Models in the Convex Case,” *Journal of Statistical Physics* **74** (1994), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF02186817). Supplies the exact covariance representation through the inverse one-form Witten operator, the nonlinear replacement for a bare hidden Hessian inverse.
- Alexander Kolesnikov and Emanuel Milman, “Brascamp--Lieb-Type Inequalities on Weighted Riemannian Manifolds with Boundary,” *Journal of Geometric Analysis* **27** (2017), [arXiv:1310.2526](https://arxiv.org/abs/1310.2526). Gives Riemannian inverse weighted-curvature covariance bounds under explicit geometric and boundary hypotheses.
- Dario Cordero-Erausquin, Robert McCann, and Michael Schmuckenschlager, “Prekopa--Leindler Type Inequalities on Riemannian Manifolds, Jacobi Fields, and Optimal Transport,” *Annales de la Faculte des Sciences de Toulouse* **15** (2006), [journal PDF](https://www.numdam.org/item/AFST_2006_6_15_4_613_0.pdf). Shows that curved marginal/transport analogues carry Ricci and Jacobian corrections; flat affine Prekopa cannot be copied into compact-group exponential coordinates.
- Roland Bauerschmidt and Thierry Bodineau, [[library/spectral-gap-critical-exponent-for-glauber-dynamics-of-hierarchical-spin-models/inq|“Spectral Gap Critical Exponent for Glauber Dynamics of Hierarchical Spin Models,”]] *Communications in Mathematical Physics* **373** (2020), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00220-019-03553-x), [arXiv:1809.02075](https://arxiv.org/abs/1809.02075). Recursively bounds Brascamp--Lieb inverse-gap forms along a hierarchical covariance decomposition and also shows critical examples whose gaps retain free-field finite-size decay; RG recursion is not itself a floor.
- Tony Lelievre, “A General Two-Scale Criteria for Logarithmic Sobolev Inequalities,” *Journal of Functional Analysis* **256** (2009), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jfa.2008.09.019), [author PDF](https://cermics.enpc.fr/cermics-rapports-recherche/2008/CERMICS-2008/CERMICS-382.pdf). Extends the transfer to nonlinear smooth coarse variables with uniform fiber and coarse bounds and controlled local mean force.
- Tadeusz Balaban's lattice gauge series develops the ultraviolet side of the proposed crossover: [gauge-covariant averaging](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01211042), [regular configurations and gauge fixing](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01466594), [the variational background field](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01229381), [background-field propagators](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01240355), [four-dimensional small-field RG](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01215223), [small-field cluster expansion](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01239022), [complete effective densities](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01217741), [Large Field I](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01257412), and [Large Field II](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01238433). The convergence claim for complete effective densities is scoped to superrenormalizable models; this series does not provide the missing gauge-invariant infinite-volume continuum gap theorem in four dimensions.
- Hao Shen, Rongchan Zhu, and Xiangchan Zhu, “A Stochastic Analysis Approach to Lattice Yang--Mills at Strong Coupling,” *Communications in Mathematical Physics* **400** (2023), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00220-022-04609-1), [arXiv:2204.12737](https://arxiv.org/abs/2204.12737). Proves volume-uniform Poincare and logarithmic-Sobolev bounds for the Langevin generator at explicit strong coupling and separately proves fixed-spacing Euclidean correlation decay; a physical Hamiltonian conclusion still requires reflection-positive transfer reconstruction.
- Ricardo S. Schor, [[library/existence-of-glueballs-in-strongly-coupled-lattice-gauge-theories/inq|“Existence of Glueballs in Strongly Coupled Lattice Gauge Theories,”]] *Nuclear Physics B* **222** (1983), 71--82, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1016/0550-3213(83)90609-0). Proves an isolated simple pole in plaquette--plaquette correlations at sufficiently large coupling and the corresponding isolated one-particle glueball state; this is a fixed-regulator strong-coupling result, not the asymptotically free continuum theorem.
- Luis Caffarelli and Luis Silvestre, “An Extension Problem Related to the Fractional Laplacian,” *Communications in Partial Differential Equations* **32** (2007), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1080/03605300600987306), [arXiv:math/0608640](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0608640). Realizes the square-root Laplacian as a Dirichlet-to-Neumann map and supplies the canonical local-bulk/nonlocal-boundary model.
- Roberto H. Schonmann, “Projections of Gibbs Measures May Be Non-Gibbsian,” *Communications in Mathematical Physics* **124** (1989), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01218465). Supplies a rigorous counterexample to the inference that a local bulk Gibbs measure must induce a Gibbsian lower-dimensional marginal.
- Fabio Cipriani and Jean-Luc Sauvageot, “Derivations as Square Roots of Dirichlet Forms,” *Journal of Functional Analysis* **201** (2003), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1016/S0022-1236(03)00085-5). Relates tracially symmetric noncommutative Dirichlet forms to closable derivations and bimodules.
- Melchior Wirth, [[library/christensen-evans-theorem-and-extensions-of-gns-symmetric-quantum-markov-semigroups/inq|“Christensen–Evans theorem and extensions of GNS-symmetric quantum Markov semigroups,”]] *Journal of Functional Analysis* **287** (2024), 110475, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jfa.2024.110475), [arXiv:2203.00341](https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.00341). Proves a refined generator theorem and GNS-symmetric extension results; its modularly covariant setting supports the finite-dimensional BKM--GNS carrier comparison but supplies neither physical time nor a Poincare gap.
- Melchior Wirth, [[library/the-differential-structure-of-generators-of-gns-symmetric-quantum-markov-semigroups/inq|“The Differential Structure of Generators of GNS-symmetric Quantum Markov Semigroups,”]] [arXiv:2207.09247](https://arxiv.org/abs/2207.09247). Represents GNS-symmetric Markov generators as squares of derivations and introduces Tomita bimodules for the modularly twisted nontracial calculus; the Type-III score-map domains and physical coverage remain additional obligations.
- Melchior Wirth, [[library/the-kms-and-gns-spectral-gap-of-quantum-markov-semigroups/inq|“The KMS and GNS Spectral Gap of Quantum Markov Semigroups,”]] [arXiv:2604.21630v1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.21630v1) (2026, recent preprint). Proves that a GNS decay rate for a quantum Markov semigroup with faithful normal invariant state on an arbitrary von Neumann algebra transfers to every normalized operator-monotone observable norm, including BKM; it does not identify that observable norm with the inverse BKM metric on state tangents or the Markov parameter with clock time.
- Matthijs Vernooij and Melchior Wirth, [[library/derivations-and-kms-symmetric-quantum-markov-semigroups/inq|“Derivations and KMS-Symmetric Quantum Markov Semigroups,”]] *Communications in Mathematical Physics* **403** (2023), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00220-023-04795-6), [arXiv:2303.15949](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.15949). Extends first-order derivation factorization to KMS-symmetric semigroups; \(\mathcal L=\delta^*\delta\) gives nonnegativity, not a Poincare lower bound.
- Melchior Wirth, “Modular Completely Dirichlet Forms as Squares of Derivations,” *International Mathematics Research Notices* (2024), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1093/imrn/rnae092). Extends the derivation route to nontracial modular settings relevant to Type III standard forms.
- Fred Cooper, Avinash Khare, and Uday Sukhatme, [[library/supersymmetry-and-quantum-mechanics/inq|“Supersymmetry and Quantum Mechanics,”]] *Physics Reports* **251** (1995), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1016/0370-1573(94)00080-M), [arXiv:hep-th/9405029](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/9405029). Supplies the first-order factorization and \(\tanh\) Pöschl--Teller partner used by the scale-shadow calculation.
- Constantine Callias, [[library/axial-anomalies-and-index-theorems-on-open-spaces/inq|“Axial Anomalies and Index Theorems on Open Spaces,”]] *Communications in Mathematical Physics* **62** (1978), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01202525). Supplies the open-space Fredholm precedent in which asymptotic invertibility controls an index.
- Jianfeng Lu, Alexander B. Watson, and Michael I. Weinstein, [[library/dirac-operators-and-domain-walls/inq|“Dirac Operators and Domain Walls,”]] *SIAM Journal on Mathematical Analysis* **52** (2020), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1137/19M127416X), [arXiv:1808.01378](https://arxiv.org/abs/1808.01378). Gives a rigorous domain-wall zero-mode and index-stability precedent for asymptotically gapped Dirac operators.
- Roman Jackiw and Claudio Rebbi, [[library/solitons-with-fermion-number-one-half/inq|“Solitons with Fermion Number 1/2,”]] *Physical Review D* **13** (1976), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevD.13.3398). Supplies the physical precedent of a localized Dirac zero mode tied to nontrivial soliton data; it does not imply a bosonic Yang--Mills gap.
- Sidney Coleman, [[library/q-balls/inq|“Q-Balls,”]] *Nuclear Physics B* **262** (1985), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1016/0550-3213(85)90286-X). Proves existence and stability conditions for charge-constrained nontopological solitons and computes low-lying excitations; its spacetime field, global \(U(1)\), potential, charge, and phase frequency are supplied structures.
- John Archibald Wheeler, [[library/geons/inq|“Geons,”]] *Physical Review* **97** (1955), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRev.97.511). Constructs classical models of electromagnetic radiation confined for many characteristic periods by its own gravity and analyzes leakage and instability; the construction already assumes Lorentzian geometry, Einstein dynamics, \(G\), and a clock.
- Michel Bonnefont, Aldéric Joulin, and Yutao Ma, [[library/a-note-on-spectral-gap-and-weighted-poincare-inequalities-for-some-one-dimensional-diffusions/inq|“A Note on Spectral Gap and Weighted Poincare Inequalities for Some One-Dimensional Diffusions,”]] *ESAIM: Probability and Statistics* **20** (2016), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1051/ps/2015019), [arXiv:1411.5982](https://arxiv.org/abs/1411.5982). Identifies the one-dimensional weighted Poincare constant with the diffusion spectral gap and supplies the generalized-Cauchy no-gap contrast.
- Kazuhiro Kuwae and Takashi Shioya, “Convergence of Spectral Structures,” *Communications in Analysis and Geometry* **11** (2003), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.4310/CAG.2003.v11.n4.a1). Supplies generalized Mosco convergence on varying Hilbert spaces.
- Andrew Lesniewski and Mary Beth Ruskai, “Monotone Riemannian Metrics and Relative Entropy on Noncommutative Probability Spaces,” *Journal of Mathematical Physics* **40** (1999), [arXiv:math-ph/9808016](https://arxiv.org/abs/math-ph/9808016). Supplies the relative-modular formulation of monotone metrics and the distinction between state-tangent and dual observable pairings.
- Denes Petz, “Monotone Metrics on Matrix Spaces,” *Linear Algebra and its Applications* **244** (1996), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1016/0024-3795(94)00211-8). Classifies a family of monotone quantum metrics, so monotonicity alone does not uniquely select BKM.
- Eric Carlen and Jan Maas, “Gradient Flow and Entropy Inequalities for Quantum Markov Semigroups with Detailed Balance,” *Journal of Functional Analysis* **273** (2017), [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/1609.01254). Shows in finite dimensions how detailed-balance Markov generators, noncommutative differential calculus, entropy gradient flow, and quantitative decay inequalities fit together; it does not identify Markov time with Lorentzian time.
- Melchior Wirth, “Exponential Relative Entropy Decay Along Quantum Markov Semigroups,” [arXiv:2505.07549](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.07549). Extends exponential Araki-relative-entropy decay, modified logarithmic-Sobolev inequalities, and an intertwining criterion to general von Neumann algebras; the semigroup and its normalization remain supplied data.

### Type III, modular transport, and expectations

- Uffe Haagerup, [[library/the-standard-form-of-von-neumann-algebras/inq|“The Standard Form of von Neumann Algebras,”]] *Mathematica Scandinavica* **37** (1975), 271--283, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.7146/math.scand.a-11606), [primary PDF](https://www.mscand.dk/article/viewFile/11606/9622). Proves that a normal isomorphism of standard forms has a unique cone-preserving unitary implementation. It canonically transports the algebraic frame and the pushed-forward state vector, not an independently chosen target state.
- Amine Marrakchi and Stefaan Vaes, [[library/ergodic-states-on-type-iii-1-factors-and-ergodic-actions/inq|“Ergodic States on Type III\(_1\) Factors and Ergodic Actions,”]] *Journal für die Reine und Angewandte Mathematik* **809** (2024), 247--260, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1515/crelle-2024-0007), [arXiv:2305.14217](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.14217). Prove that a nontrivial von Neumann algebra with separable predual admits a faithful normal state with scalar centralizer exactly when it is a type-III\(_1\) factor, with such states dense \(G_\delta\); the associated modular action is weakly mixing, not thereby spectrally gapped.
- Roberto Longo, [[library/a-bekenstein-type-bound-in-qft/inq|“A Bekenstein-Type Bound in QFT,”]] *Communications in Mathematical Physics* **406** (2025), article 95, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00220-025-05261-1), [arXiv:2409.14408](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.14408). Proves the finite-width upper bound of regional vacuum relative entropy by \(2\pi R\) times physical Hamiltonian energy in natural units. The differentiated Hessian and descent-loss gap criterion in this workspace is a new deduction requiring explicit smoothness and form-core hypotheses.
- Alain Connes and Henri Moscovici, [[library/type-iii-and-spectral-triples/inq|“Type III and Spectral Triples,”]] [arXiv:math/0609703](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0609703). Introduces twisted commutators for natural Type III examples and constructs the ordinary cyclic Chern character for finitely summable twisted triples; the twist remains invertible and supplies no energy floor.
- Alain Connes and Carlo Rovelli, [[library/von-neumann-algebra-automorphisms-and-the-time-thermodynamics-relation/inq|“Von Neumann Algebra Automorphisms and Time-Thermodynamics Relation in Generally Covariant Quantum Theories,”]] *Classical and Quantum Gravity* **11** (1994), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1088/0264-9381/11/12/007), [arXiv:gr-qc/9406019](https://arxiv.org/abs/gr-qc/9406019). Proposes state-relative modular flow as thermal time; the flow is a reversible automorphism group and its outer class is not an irreversible fact-order or a mass scale.
- Agostino Devastato, Shane Farnsworth, Fedele Lizzi, and Pierre Martinetti, [[library/lorentz-signature-and-twisted-spectral-triples/inq|“Lorentz Signature and Twisted Spectral Triples,”]] *Journal of High Energy Physics* **03** (2018) 089, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/JHEP03(2018)089), [arXiv:1710.04965](https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.04965). In a specific minimal-twist/Standard-Model construction, relates the twist to a Krein product and Wick rotation; it is not a general derivation of irreversible order or clock dynamics from an arbitrary twist.
- Ali H. Chamseddine and Alain Connes, [[library/the-spectral-action-principle/inq|“The Spectral Action Principle,”]] *Communications in Mathematical Physics* **186** (1997), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/s002200050126), [arXiv:hep-th/9606001](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/9606001). Defines the fermionic-plus-spectral action for a supplied spectral triple; the even bosonic trace is invariant under \(D\mapsto-D\) and does not select an orientation.
- Bram Mesland and Adam Rennie, [[library/the-friedrichs-angle-and-alternating-projections-in-hilbert-c-star-modules/inq|“The Friedrichs Angle and Alternating Projections in Hilbert \(C^*\)-Modules,”]] *Journal of Mathematical Analysis and Applications* **516** (2022) 126474, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jmaa.2022.126474), [arXiv:2112.03822](https://arxiv.org/abs/2112.03822). Supplies the closed-sum and alternating-projection theorem whose Hilbert-space specialization gives the exact two-descent angle floor.
- Masamichi Takesaki, “Conditional Expectations in von Neumann Algebras,” *Journal of Functional Analysis* **9** (1972), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1016/0022-1236(72)90004-3). Gives the modular-invariance criterion for a preserving normal expectation relative to a faithful normal semifinite weight, with semifiniteness of its restriction; the faithful-normal-state case used here is the bounded specialization.
- Julien Deschamps, Franco Fagnola, Emanuela Sasso, and Veronica Umanita, [[library/structure-of-uniformly-continuous-quantum-markov-semigroups/inq|“Structure of Uniformly Continuous Quantum Markov Semigroups,”]] *Reviews in Mathematical Physics* **28** (2016), 1650003, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1142/S0129055X16500033), [arXiv:1412.3239](https://arxiv.org/abs/1412.3239). Characterize decoherence-free algebras and noiseless subsystems; their multiplicative-domain results support the faithful-descent rigidity deduction used here.
- Luigi Accardi and Carlo Cecchini, [[library/conditional-expectations-and-a-theorem-of-takesaki/inq|“Conditional Expectations in von Neumann Algebras and a Theorem of Takesaki,”]] *Journal of Functional Analysis* **45** (1982), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1016/0022-1236(82)90022-2). Supplies the state-dependent generalized expectation and its standard-form contraction when an ordinary preserving expectation is unavailable.
- Denes Petz, [[library/sufficient-subalgebras-and-relative-entropy/inq|“Sufficient Subalgebras and the Relative Entropy of States of a von Neumann Algebra,”]] *Communications in Mathematical Physics* **105** (1986), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01212345), [open PDF](https://math.bme.hu/~petz/pdf/29suff.pdf). Proves that equality of relative entropy under restriction is equivalent, under the stated hypotheses, to retaining the Connes cocycle; it determines the exact zero-loss state pairs but no positive minimum loss.
- Thomas Faulkner and Stefan Hollands, [[library/approximate-recoverability-and-relative-entropy-ii/inq|“Approximate Recoverability and Relative Entropy II,”]] *Letters in Mathematical Physics* **112** (2022), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11005-022-01510-9), [arXiv:2010.05513](https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.05513). Supplies the general-von-Neumann strengthened data-processing theorem and, for a state-preserving expectation, the exact chain rule that makes restriction loss purely vertical.
- Li Gao, Marius Junge, and Nicholas LaRacuente, [[library/relative-entropy-and-subalgebra-index/inq|“Relative Entropy for von Neumann Subalgebras,”]] *International Journal of Mathematics* **31** (2020), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1142/S0129167X20500469), [arXiv:1909.01906](https://arxiv.org/abs/1909.01906). Identifies the Pimsner--Popa index with a supremal relative-entropy capacity in finite-dimensional algebras and \(II_1\) factors; this is a maximum-loss result, not a positive minimum.
- Roberto Longo and Edward Witten, [[library/a-note-on-continuous-entropy/inq|“A Note on Continuous Entropy,”]] *Pure and Applied Mathematics Quarterly* **19** (2023), 2501--2523, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.4310/PAMQ.2023.v19.n5.a5), [arXiv:2202.03357](https://arxiv.org/abs/2202.03357). Proves that the log index is the optimal relative-entropy loss for finite-index inclusions of infinite-dimensional factors and records the exact dual-expectation certainty identity; it supplies a maximum distinction capacity, not a lower spectral edge.
- Fumio Hiai, [[library/minimum-index-for-subfactors-and-entropy-ii/inq|“Minimum Index for Subfactors and Entropy II,”]] *Journal of the Mathematical Society of Japan* **43** (1991), 347--379, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.2969/jmsj/04320347). Defines a distinct ensemble/decomposition inclusion entropy; for an infinite subfactor it reaches the expectation's log index exactly on the minimal-expectation branch. This functional must not be substituted for Longo--Witten's direct-state supremum.
- Feng Xu, [[library/on-relative-entropy-and-global-index/inq|“On Relative Entropy and Global Index,”]] *Transactions of the American Mathematical Society* **373** (2020), 1421--1453, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1090/tran/7989), [arXiv:1812.01119](https://arxiv.org/abs/1812.01119). Derives the relative-entropy duality used in the expectation/commutant certainty identity and relates its failure in conformal nets to global index.
- Javier M. Magan and Diego Pontello, [[library/quantum-complementarity-through-entropic-certainty-principles/inq|“Quantum Complementarity through Entropic Certainty Principles,”]] *Physical Review A* **103** (2021), 012211, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevA.103.012211), [arXiv:2005.01760](https://arxiv.org/abs/2005.01760). Gives the finite-dimensional complementary-observable formulation in which two relative entropies sum to log index and derives uncertainty bounds by monotonicity.
- Luca Giorgetti, [[library/planar-algebraic-conditional-expectations/inq|“A Planar Algebraic Description of Conditional Expectations,”]] *International Journal of Mathematics* **34** (2023), [arXiv:2111.04488](https://arxiv.org/abs/2111.04488). For properly infinite algebras, reconstructs finite-index expectations from conjugate solutions and Q-systems and supplies the normalized intertwiner used in the exact square-response theorem.
- Arthur Bartels, Christopher L. Douglas, and Andre Henriques, [[library/dualizability-and-index-of-subfactors/inq|“Dualizability and Index of Subfactors,”]] *Quantum Topology* **5** (2014), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.4171/QT/53), [arXiv:1110.5671](https://arxiv.org/abs/1110.5671). Identifies the intrinsic/minimal subfactor index with squared statistical dimension and proves multiplicativity under Connes fusion in a framework applying to Type I, II, and III algebras; its Pimsner--Popa order bound is not an \(L^2\) frame bound.
- Masaki Izumi, Roberto Longo, and Sorin Popa, [[library/a-galois-correspondence-for-compact-groups-of-automorphisms/inq|“A Galois Correspondence for Compact Groups of Automorphisms of von Neumann Algebras,”]] *Journal of Functional Analysis* **155** (1998), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1006/jfan.1997.3228), [arXiv:funct-an/9604004](https://arxiv.org/abs/funct-an/9604004). Supplies the minimal compact-group fixed-point decomposition into sectors indexed by every irreducible representation, making the continuous-group fixed-point inclusion infinite-index.
- Marcel Bischoff, Simone Del Vecchio, and Luca Giorgetti, [[library/compact-hypergroups-from-discrete-subfactors/inq|“Compact Hypergroups from Discrete Subfactors,”]] *Journal of Functional Analysis* **281** (2021), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jfa.2021.109004), [arXiv:2007.12384](https://arxiv.org/abs/2007.12384). Reconstructs state-preserving UCP hypergroup actions for discrete Type-III subfactors and identifies the smaller algebra as their common fixed algebra; the finite-index/finite-dual-sum case gives a compact finite hypergroup, not a vacuum-only physical action.
- Marcel Bischoff, Simone Del Vecchio, and Luca Giorgetti, [[library/quantum-operations-on-conformal-nets/inq|“Quantum Operations on Conformal Nets,”]] *Reviews in Mathematical Physics* **35** (2023), 2350007, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1142/S0129055X23500071), [arXiv:2204.14105](https://arxiv.org/abs/2204.14105). Construct compatible, vacuum-preserving, conformally covariant UCP operation families on chiral conformal nets and organize those fixing discrete subnets into compact hypergroups; this is a process-net precedent, not a four-dimensional Yang--Mills construction.
- Horacio Casini, Marina Huerta, Javier M. Magan, and Diego Pontello, [[library/entropic-order-parameters-for-the-phases-of-qft/inq|“Entropic Order Parameters for the Phases of QFT,”]] *Journal of High Energy Physics* **04** (2021), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/JHEP04(2021)277), [arXiv:2008.11748](https://arxiv.org/abs/2008.11748). Supplies the additivity--Haag-duality regional inclusions, the four-dimensional ring/complement topology, the \(Z(G)^*\times Z(G)\) nonlocal class group, and the \(2\log|Z(G)|\) certainty relation.
- Ofer Aharony, Nathan Seiberg, and Yuji Tachikawa, [[library/reading-between-the-lines-of-four-dimensional-gauge-theories/inq|“Reading between the Lines of Four-Dimensional Gauge Theories,”]] *Journal of High Energy Physics* **08** (2013) 115, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/JHEP08(2013)115), [arXiv:1305.0318](https://arxiv.org/abs/1305.0318). Classifies mutually local electric--magnetic line spectra and the alternating charge pairing; it supplies oriented flux incidence, not an energy gap.
- Horacio Casini, Javier M. Magan, and Pedro J. Martinez, [[library/entropic-order-parameters-in-weakly-coupled-gauge-theories/inq|“Entropic Order Parameters in Weakly Coupled Gauge Theories,”]] *Journal of High Energy Physics* **01** (2022), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/JHEP01(2022)079), [arXiv:2110.02980](https://arxiv.org/abs/2110.02980). Computes the scale-dependent approach to finite topological saturation in weakly coupled gauge theories and explicitly distinguishes finite subgroup expectations from continuous infinite-index class groups.
- Davide Gaiotto, Anton Kapustin, Nathan Seiberg, and Brian Willett, [[library/generalized-global-symmetries/inq|“Generalized Global Symmetries,”]] *Journal of High Energy Physics* **02** (2015), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/JHEP02(2015)172), [arXiv:1412.5148](https://arxiv.org/abs/1412.5148). Supplies the one-form symmetry framework distinguishing finite center flux classes from continuous local gauge redundancy.
- Sorin Popa, [[library/orthogonal-pairs-of-star-subalgebras-in-finite-von-neumann-algebras/inq|“Orthogonal Pairs of \(*\)-Subalgebras in Finite von Neumann Algebras,”]] *Journal of Operator Theory* **9** (1983), [record](https://jot.theta.ro/jot/archive/1983-009-002/1983-009-002-003.html), [PDF](https://jot.theta.ro/jot/archive/1983-009-002/1983-009-002-003.pdf). Supplies the finite tracial commuting-square precedent: products of the corresponding \(L^2\)-projections are intersection projections under the orthogonality condition. The present faithful-state construction imposes cross-commutation separately; Takesaki existence does not imply it.
- Alain Connes, “Une classification des facteurs de type III,” *Annales scientifiques de l'École Normale Supérieure* **6** (1973), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.24033/asens.1247). Supplies the cocycle law and type-\(\mathrm{III}_1\) modular-spectrum invariant used to rule out a positive gapped Hamiltonian from one modular logarithm.
- Gert K. Pedersen and Masamichi Takesaki, “The Radon--Nikodym Theorem for von Neumann Algebras,” *Acta Mathematica* **130** (1973), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF02392262). In the commuting case represents a cocycle group by \(h^{it}\); state normalization then makes a semidefinite \(\log h\) trivial.
- Tony Falcone and Masamichi Takesaki, “The Non-commutative Flow of Weights on a Von Neumann Algebra,” *Journal of Functional Analysis* **182** (2001), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1006/jfan.2000.3718). Constructs the canonical semifinite core and trace-scaling action.
- Helmut Reeh and Siegfried Schlieder, “Bemerkungen zur Unitäräquivalenz von Lorentzinvarianten Feldern,” *Nuovo Cimento* **22** (1961), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF02787889). Supplies the cyclicity theorem used in the local-expectation no-go; separation follows from locality plus cyclicity for a spacelike complement in the usual vacuum setting.
- Jakob Yngvason, “The Role of Type III Factors in Quantum Field Theory,” [arXiv:math-ph/0411058](https://arxiv.org/abs/math-ph/0411058). Reviews why Type III factors occur naturally and why net embeddings, not the abstract isomorphism type of one factor, distinguish theories.
- Ian Koot, [[library/relative-positions-of-half-sided-modular-inclusions/inq|“Relative Positions of Half-Sided Modular Inclusions,”]] *Letters in Mathematical Physics* **115** (2025), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11005-025-02008-w), [arXiv:2503.18036](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18036). Characterizes relative positions inside one standard subspace and shows why separate HSMIs do not by themselves yield commuting translation groups.
- Ralf Kähler and Hans-Werner Wiesbrock, [[library/modular-theory-and-the-reconstruction-of-four-dimensional-quantum-field-theories/inq|“Modular Theory and the Reconstruction of Four-Dimensional Quantum Field Theories,”]] *Journal of Mathematical Physics* **42** (2001), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1063/1.1327597). Shows that a sufficiently rigid modular constellation can reconstruct a \(3+1\)-dimensional Poincare representation and local net; it supplies kinematics, not a positive Casimir floor.
- Romeo Brunetti, Daniele Guido, and Roberto Longo, [[library/modular-localization-and-wigner-particles/inq|“Modular Localization and Wigner Particles,”]] *Reviews in Mathematical Physics* **14** (2002), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1142/S0129055X02001387), [arXiv:math-ph/0203021](https://arxiv.org/abs/math-ph/0203021). Constructs modularly localized nets from a supplied positive-energy Poincare representation, so its mass and spin labels are inputs rather than consequences of local Type III structure.
- Stefan Hollands and Robert Wald, “Local Wick Polynomials and Time Ordered Products of Quantum Fields in Curved Spacetime,” *Communications in Mathematical Physics* **223** (2001), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/s002200100540), [arXiv:gr-qc/0103074](https://arxiv.org/abs/gr-qc/0103074). Types the ultraviolet renormalization problem through local covariant products and scaling at coincident points; a bounded causal patch is not a substitute for this short-distance construction.

### Measurement, context, and factivity

- Hazime Mori, [[library/transport-collective-motion-and-brownian-motion/inq|“Transport, Collective Motion, and Brownian Motion,”]] *Progress of Theoretical Physics* **33** (1965), 423--455, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1143/PTP.33.423). Derives an exact generalized Langevin description by projecting microscopic dynamics onto selected variables; the orthogonal dynamics produces memory and fluctuating-force terms. This licenses deterministic hidden-memory precedents, not stochastic ontology or a Yang--Mills energy gap.
- John G. Kemeny and J. Laurie Snell, *Finite Markov Chains* (1960), supplies the strong-lumpability criterion distinguishing an autonomous Markov factor from a generic hidden-memory projection.
- “A Counterexample to the Jacobian Conjecture” (2026), [primary manuscript](https://www.ulam.ai/research/jacobian.pdf). Verifies the map announced by Levent Alpöge with determinant $-2$ and generic degree three, and identifies its finite étale inverse cover and nonproperness set.
- Shuhong Gao, “Counterexamples to the Jacobian Conjecture in Dimensions Greater Than Two” (2026), [arXiv:2608.00222](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00222). Gives a self-contained tangent-sweep construction and new examples, including a distinct three-dimensional map of determinant $+2$ and generic degree four; it is used here only as a local--global inversion precedent.
- Denes Petz, “Sufficiency of Channels over von Neumann Algebras,” *The Quarterly Journal of Mathematics* **39** (1988), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1093/qmath/39.1.97). Characterizes sufficiency and state-dependent recovery for channels; in the present construction it controls the zero set of relative-entropy loss, not actuality or energy.
- E. B. Davies and J. T. Lewis, “An Operational Approach to Quantum Probability,” *Communications in Mathematical Physics* **17** (1970), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01647093). Defines the instrument framework for joint and conditional outcome probabilities; it does not supply which outcome actually occurs.
- Masanao Ozawa, “Quantum Measurement, Information, and Completely Positive Maps,” in *Quantum Communication, Computing, and Measurement 3* (2002), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/0-306-47114-0_15), [arXiv:quant-ph/0107090](https://arxiv.org/abs/quant-ph/0107090). Supplies the completely positive instrument grammar and the distinction between outcome statistics and posterior state changes; it does not provide an actuality selector or Hamiltonian gap.
- Gelfand duality for commutative unital $C^*$-algebras supplies the exact identification of points with characters. The workspace application in [[sufficient-reason/facticity-and-pointing|facticity and pointing]] is used only after a readout context has been declared; it is not a global valuation theorem for a noncommutative observable algebra.

### Cosmological fossil diagnostics

- Ken'ichi Saikawa and Satoshi Shirai, “Primordial Gravitational Waves, Precisely: The Role of Thermodynamics in the Standard Model,” *Journal of Cosmology and Astroparticle Physics* 2018 (05), 035, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1088/1475-7516/2018/05/035), [arXiv:1803.01038](https://arxiv.org/abs/1803.01038), and [machine-readable equation-of-state table](https://member.ipmu.jp/satoshi.shirai/standardmodel2018.dat). Supplies the distinct \(g_{*\rho}\) and \(g_{*s}\) values used in the conditional \(46.27\,\mathrm{MeV}\) horizon and trace diagnostics.
- Daniel J. Eisenstein and Wayne Hu, “Baryonic Features in the Matter Transfer Function,” *The Astrophysical Journal* **496** (1998), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1086/305424), [arXiv:astro-ph/9709112](https://arxiv.org/abs/astro-ph/9709112). Separates the physical ingredients of the baryonic transfer feature and treats the drag ruler as an accumulated sound horizon.
- Planck Collaboration, “Planck 2018 Results. VI. Cosmological Parameters,” *Astronomy & Astrophysics* **641** (2020), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1051/0004-6361/201833910), [arXiv:1807.06209](https://arxiv.org/abs/1807.06209). Gives the base-flat-$\Lambda$CDM TT,TE,EE+lowE+lensing inference $r_{\mathrm{drag}}=147.09\pm0.26\,\mathrm{Mpc}$; BAO observations constrain distance ratios involving that calibrated ruler.
- Wayne Hu, David N. Spergel, and Martin White, “Distinguishing Causal Seeds from Inflation,” *Physical Review D* **55** (1997), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevD.55.3288), [arXiv:astro-ph/9605193](https://arxiv.org/abs/astro-ph/9605193). Shows that generic active causal stress seeds have distinguishable isocurvature peak phases and heights, supplying a concrete test for any late mass-engagement source.
- A. Bazavov et al. (HotQCD Collaboration), “The Equation of State in (2+1)-Flavor QCD,” *Physical Review D* **90** (2014), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevD.90.094503), [arXiv:1407.6387](https://arxiv.org/abs/1407.6387). Places the physical-mass chiral crossover band at about $145$--$163\,\mathrm{MeV}$, excluding a numerical identification of the $46.27\,\mathrm{MeV}$ inverse grain with that crossover.
- L. M. Satarov, I. N. Mishustin, and W. Greiner, [[library/evolution-of-antibaryon-abundances-in-the-early-universe-and-in-heavy-ion-collisions/inq|“Evolution of Antibaryon Abundances in the Early Universe and in Heavy-Ion Collisions,”]] *Physical Review C* **88** (2013), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevC.88.024908), [arXiv:1305.4046](https://arxiv.org/abs/1305.4046). Supplies a kinetic model in which the symmetric baryon--antibaryon population drains while the small conserved net baryon excess survives.
- Inga Kuznetsova and Johann Rafelski, [[library/unstable-hadrons-in-hot-hadron-gas/inq|“Unstable Hadrons in Hot Hadron Gas: In the Laboratory and in the Early Universe,”]] *Physical Review C* **82** (2010), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevC.82.035203), [arXiv:1002.0375](https://arxiv.org/abs/1002.0375). Treats decay and inverse formation together and applies the resulting relaxation rates to primordial pion and muon abundances.
- Cheng Tao Yang and Johann Rafelski, [[library/cosmological-strangeness-abundance/inq|“Cosmological Strangeness Abundance,”]] *Physics Letters B* **827** (2022), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.physletb.2022.136944), [arXiv:2108.01752](https://arxiv.org/abs/2108.01752). Finds distinct reaction-dependent loss-of-balance temperatures rather than one universal hadronic freeze-out.

### Relativistic sectors, uncertainty, and symmetry

- E. Wigner, “On Unitary Representations of the Inhomogeneous Lorentz Group,” *Annals of Mathematics* **40** (1939), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.2307/1968551), [primary scan](https://www.math.utoronto.ca/mgualt/courses/25-QM/docs/Wigner-1939.pdf). Classifies positive-energy elementary relativistic sectors through translation orbits and little-group representations.
- Krzysztof Andrzejewski, Cezary Gonera, Joanna Gonera, Piotr Kosiński, and Paweł Maślanka, “Spinning particles, coadjoint orbits and Hamiltonian formalism,” *Nuclear Physics B* **975** (2022), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.nuclphysb.2022.115664), [arXiv:2008.09478](https://arxiv.org/abs/2008.09478). Gives an explicit massive Poincare coadjoint-orbit carrier, constrained Hamiltonian description, and quantization.
- H. P. Robertson, “The Uncertainty Principle,” *Physical Review* **34** (1929), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRev.34.163). Gives the commutator lower bound for general Hermitian observables; the special status of position and momentum comes from their canonical commutator, not a restriction of uncertainty to momentum.
- Jeffrey Goldstone, Abdus Salam, and Steven Weinberg, “Broken Symmetries,” *Physical Review* **127** (1962), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRev.127.965). Under the stated continuous-global-symmetry hypotheses, a noninvariant vacuum entails massless spin-zero excitations and therefore supplies a decisive counterexample to selection implying a gap.
- S. Elitzur, “Impossibility of Spontaneously Breaking Local Symmetries,” *Physical Review D* **12** (1975), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevD.12.3978). Proves in the unfixed lattice setting that a local gauge symmetry cannot spontaneously break in the ordinary gauge-variant sense.
- Ichiro Yokota, [[library/exceptional-lie-groups-yokota/inq|*Exceptional Lie Groups*]], [arXiv:0902.0431](https://arxiv.org/abs/0902.0431), Section 2.12. Constructs the real order-three Albert-algebra automorphism \(w\), identifies its fixed algebra as \(\mathfrak h_3(\mathbb C)\), and proves \(C_{F_4}(w)\cong(SU(3)\times SU(3))/\mathbb Z_3\).
- Richard Slansky, [[library/group-theory-for-unified-model-building/inq|“Group Theory for Unified Model Building,”]] *Physics Reports* **79** (1981), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1016/0370-1573(81)90092-2). Table 46 supplies the \(F_4\downarrow SU(3)\times SU(3)\) branching used, with the displayed \(SU(2)\times U(1)\) restriction, to derive the 149-dimensional normal-module decomposition.
- John C. Baez and Paul Schwahn, [[library/standard-model-from-exceptional-jordan-algebra/inq|“The Standard Model Gauge Group from the Exceptional Jordan Algebra,”]] [arXiv:2606.15235](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.15235). Prove that the stabilizer of \(\mathfrak h_2(\mathbb C)\) intersected with the identity component of the stabilizer of \(\mathfrak h_3(\mathbb C)\) inside \(\mathfrak h_3(\mathbb O)\) is \(S(U(2)\times U(3))\), and record both \(\mathbb O\cong\mathbb C\oplus\mathbb C^3\) under the fixed-slice color group and the vector/spinor/spinor triality action on the three Jordan off-diagonal slots. These are symmetry-after-selection and carrier statements, not a dynamical selection or mass-gap theorem.
- Ádám Gyenge, [[library/transition-function-of-g2-over-s6/inq|“The Transition Function of \(G_2\) over \(S^6\),”]] *SIGMA* **15** (2019) 078, [DOI and source files](https://sigma-journal.com/2019/078/), [arXiv:1811.03613](https://arxiv.org/abs/1811.03613). Gives explicit local trivializations and the transition function of the principal \(SU(3)\)-bundle \(G_2\to S^6\), whose clutching class generates \(\pi_5(SU(3))\); this bundle is not a universal source of four-dimensional color-bundle sectors.
- Michael F. Atiyah and Isadore M. Singer, “The Index of Elliptic Operators. III,” *Annals of Mathematics* **87** (1968), [primary journal record and article](https://annals.math.princeton.edu/1968/87-3/p07). Supplies the cohomological index formula used to turn the generator bundle's \(c_3=\pm2\) into a twisted six-dimensional Dirac index \(\pm1\); that index is not a bosonic spectral gap.
- Moritz Doll, Ksenia Fedosova, and Anke Pohl, [[library/counting-resonances-on-hyperbolic-surfaces-with-unitary-twists/inq|“Counting Resonances on Hyperbolic Surfaces with Unitary Twists,”]] [arXiv:2109.12923](https://arxiv.org/abs/2109.12923). Constructs the positive self-adjoint flat-bundle Laplacian for unitary twists and its cusp model; the triangle-character theorem combines that standard analytic carrier with a new finite character calculation.
- Frédéric Naud and Polyxeni Spilioti, [[library/on-the-spectrum-of-twisted-laplacians-and-the-teichmuller-representation/inq|“On the Spectrum of Twisted Laplacians and the Teichmüller Representation,”]] [arXiv:2205.09540](https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.09540). Studies the non-self-adjoint operators produced by nonunitary flat twists, supplying the firewall against using the torus family's unipotent \(GL_4(\mathbb Z)\) monodromy as if it were already a positive automorphic carrier.
- Kai Behrend and Ping Xu, [[library/differentiable-stacks-and-gerbes/inq|“Differentiable Stacks and Gerbes,”]] [arXiv:math/0605694](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0605694). Gives the Morita-invariant differentiable-stack framework in which a transitive action groupoid \(G\ltimes G/H\) presents the classifying stack \(\mathbf B H\); this is the type distinction between the coarse point and the torsor-valued quotient used here.
- Urs Schreiber and Konrad Waldorf, [[library/parallel-transport-and-functors/inq|“Parallel Transport and Functors,”]] *Journal of Homotopy and Related Structures* **4** (2009), [arXiv:0705.0452](https://arxiv.org/abs/0705.0452). Characterizes bundle connections by smooth path-groupoid transport with local trivializations and descent data; this controls the passage from finite comparison arrows to continuum connection geometry without collapsing nonflat transport to the ordinary homotopy groupoid.
- Hsien-Chung Wang, [[library/invariant-connections-over-a-principal-fibre-bundle/inq|“On Invariant Connections over a Principal Fibre Bundle,”]] *Nagoya Mathematical Journal* **13** (1958), [primary article](https://www.cambridge.org/core/services/aop-cambridge-core/content/view/7AC585E9DDCEF66E15F24C3481EEF592/S0027763000023461a.pdf/on-invariant-connections-over-a-principal-fibre-bundle.pdf). Classifies invariant connections on homogeneous principal bundles; applied to \(G_2\to G_2/SU(3)\), the vanishing of \(\operatorname{Hom}_{SU(3)}(\mathbf3\oplus\bar{\mathbf3},\mathbf8)\) makes the reductive connection unique.
- Olaf Lechtenfeld and Alexander D. Popov, [[library/instantons-on-the-six-sphere-and-twistors/inq|“Instantons on the Six-Sphere and Twistors,”]] *Journal of Mathematical Physics* **53** (2012) 123506, [arXiv:1206.4128](https://arxiv.org/abs/1206.4128). Writes the canonical nearly Kähler \(SU(3)\)-instanton on \(G_2/SU(3)\); it is the distinguished torsionful characteristic connection, not the arbitrary connection space of four-dimensional Yang--Mills theory.
- Michael Atiyah, Raoul Bott, and Arnold Shapiro, [[library/clifford-modules-atiyah-bott-shapiro/inq|“Clifford Modules,”]] *Topology* **3**, Supplement 1 (1964), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1016/0040-9383(64)90003-5), [primary PDF](https://www.maths.ed.ac.uk/~v1ranick/papers/abs.pdf). Constructs KO and Thom classes from graded Clifford modules and their symbols; it distinguishes the invertible degree-eight Bott operation from taking eight ungraded copies of a representation.
- C. T. C. Wall, [[library/graded-brauer-groups-wall/inq|“Graded Brauer Groups,”]] *Journal für die reine und angewandte Mathematik* **213** (1964), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1515/crll.1964.213.187), [primary PDF](https://webhomes.maths.ed.ac.uk/~v1ranick/papers/wallbrauer.pdf). Supplies the graded-Morita classification in which real Clifford degree is periodic modulo eight; the class does not determine an unnormalized representation trace.
- A. A. Albert, [[library/a-note-on-the-exceptional-jordan-algebra/inq|“A Note on the Exceptional Jordan Algebra,”]] *Proceedings of the National Academy of Sciences* **36** (1950), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1073/pnas.36.7.372), [full text](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC1063206/). Proves the exceptional algebra is not special, blocking any nonzero product-preserving Jordan homomorphism from the simple Albert algebra into an associative operator algebra. It does not prohibit module, order, group, or Lie representations.
- Nathan Jacobson, [[library/general-representation-theory-of-jordan-algebras/inq|“General Representation Theory of Jordan Algebras,”]] *Transactions of the American Mathematical Society* **70** (1951), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1090/S0002-9947-1951-0041118-9), [primary PDF](https://www.math.uci.edu/~brusso/jacobson1951.pdf). Distinguishes Jordan modules from special-product embeddings and supplies the regular multiplication representation \(x\mapsto L_x\), which carries the finite positive Hessian into ordinary Hilbert-space operators without preserving the Jordan product by anticommutators.

### Entropy, acceleration, and dimensional calibration

- Jacob D. Bekenstein, “Black Holes and Entropy,” *Physical Review D* **7** (1973), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevD.7.2333), [APS record](https://journals.aps.org/prd/abstract/10.1103/PhysRevD.7.2333). Motivates black-hole entropy using information inaccessible to an exterior observer and formulates the generalized second law; it does not establish a universal maximum mass.
- Jacob D. Bekenstein, “Universal Upper Bound on the Entropy-to-Energy Ratio for Bounded Systems,” *Physical Review D* **23** (1981), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevD.23.287), [APS record](https://journals.aps.org/prd/abstract/10.1103/PhysRevD.23.287). Proposes \(S\leq2\pi k_BER/(\hbar c)\) for bounded systems and identifies black-hole saturation; the radius, energy, system class, and entropy notion are hypotheses, not a universal mass theorem.
- S. W. Hawking, “Particle Creation by Black Holes,” *Communications in Mathematical Physics* **43** (1975), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF02345020), [primary scan](https://astrofrelat.fcaglp.unlp.edu.ar/agujeros_negros/media/Papers/Hawking_1975-Particle_creation_by_black_holes.pdf). Derives thermal emission and fixes the Hawking temperature and quarter-area entropy coefficient.
- W. G. Unruh, [[library/notes-on-black-hole-evaporation/inq|“Notes on Black-Hole Evaporation,”]] *Physical Review D* **14** (1976), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevD.14.870). Shows with an accelerated detector that Minkowski vacuum has a thermal response at acceleration temperature; the acceleration, trajectory, field state, and clock normalization are supplied rather than a Yang--Mills scale.
- G. W. Gibbons and S. W. Hawking, [[library/cosmological-event-horizons-thermodynamics-and-particle-creation/inq|“Cosmological Event Horizons, Thermodynamics, and Particle Creation,”]] *Physical Review D* **15** (1977), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevD.15.2738). Makes horizon entropy and temperature explicitly relative to an observer's cosmological causal patch.
- Rong-Gen Cai and Sang Pyo Kim, [[library/first-law-of-thermodynamics-and-friedmann-equations-of-frw-universe/inq|“First Law of Thermodynamics and Friedmann Equations of Friedmann--Robertson--Walker Universe,”]] *Journal of High Energy Physics* **02** (2005) 050, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1088/1126-6708/2005/02/050), [arXiv:hep-th/0501055](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/0501055). Derives Friedmann equations from an apparent-horizon first-law construction while treating \(T=1/(2\pi R_A)\) as the quasi-static/canonical horizon-temperature input rather than a theorem of arbitrary dynamical equilibrium.
- H. Casini, “Relative Entropy and the Bekenstein Bound,” *Classical and Quantum Gravity* **25** (2008), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1088/0264-9381/25/20/205021), [arXiv:0804.2182](https://arxiv.org/abs/0804.2182). Retypes the finite QFT bound as positivity of vacuum-relative entropy, \(\Delta S/k_B\leq\Delta\langle K\rangle\), when the subtracted terms are separately defined; this is not a Hamiltonian lower bound.
- Ted Jacobson, “Thermodynamics of Spacetime: The Einstein Equation of State,” *Physical Review Letters* **75** (1995), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevLett.75.1260), [arXiv:gr-qc/9504004](https://arxiv.org/abs/gr-qc/9504004). Derives the Einstein equation from local Rindler heat balance after an entropy-per-area density is supplied; that density fixes Newton's coupling rather than deriving a Yang--Mills scale.
- Ted Jacobson, “Entanglement Equilibrium and the Einstein Equation,” *Physical Review Letters* **116** (2016), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevLett.116.201101), [arXiv:1505.04753](https://arxiv.org/abs/1505.04753). Recovers the leading semiclassical Einstein equation from first-order vacuum-entropy stationarity for small balls under its conformal and scale assumptions; first variation is not a coercive Hessian.
- Erik Verlinde, “On the Origin of Gravity and the Laws of Newton,” *JHEP* **04** (2011) 029, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/JHEP04(2011)029), [arXiv:1001.0785](https://arxiv.org/abs/1001.0785). Gives an entropic-force construction in which $G$ enters the holographic bit-count normalization; it is therefore not a parameter-free source of $E_*$.
- T. Padmanabhan, “Thermodynamical Aspects of Gravity: New Insights,” *Reports on Progress in Physics* **73** (2010) 046901, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1088/0034-4885/73/4/046901), [arXiv:0911.5004](https://arxiv.org/abs/0911.5004). Develops boundary, equipartition, and null-vector variational rewritings of gravitational dynamics; the gravitational entropy density and dynamics are supplied rather than microscopically derived.
- Nima Lashkari and Mark Van Raamsdonk, “Canonical Energy is Quantum Fisher Information,” *Journal of High Energy Physics* **04** (2016) 153, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/JHEP04(2016)153), [arXiv:1508.00897](https://arxiv.org/abs/1508.00897). Gives a controlled holographic equality between a regional relative-entropy Hessian and gravitational canonical energy; it proves a same-tangent solder can exist, not a uniform gap.
- B. L. Hu and Enric Verdaguer, “Stochastic Gravity: Theory and Applications,” *Living Reviews in Relativity* **11** (2008) 3, [DOI](https://doi.org/10.12942/lrr-2008-3), [arXiv:0802.0658](https://arxiv.org/abs/0802.0658). Defines the stress-tensor noise kernel and Einstein--Langevin framework; its covariance is not a Hamiltonian generator, actuality rule, or source of a new spectral threshold.
- P. Burikham, T. Harko, and M. J. Lake, [[library/the-qcd-mass-gap-and-quark-deconfinement-scales-as-mass-bounds-in-strong-gravity/inq|“The QCD Mass Gap and Quark Deconfinement Scales as Mass Bounds in Strong Gravity,”]] *European Physical Journal C* **77** (2017) 803, [arXiv:1705.11174](https://arxiv.org/abs/1705.11174). Distinguish the ordinary de Sitter cell \((\ell_P^2\ell_{\mathrm{dS}})^{1/3}\), its strong-gravity analogue \((\ell_{\mathrm{sP}}^2\ell_{\mathrm{sd}})^{1/3}\), and a separate static Buchdahl-bound route. The shared cubic class is useful provenance, but their strong-gravity mechanism assumes a glueball mass a priori and is not an \(SU(3)\) Yang--Mills gap theorem.
- Martin Enriquez-Rojo and Tobias Schroeder, [[library/asymptotic-symmetries-and-memories-of-gauge-theories-in-flrw-spacetimes/inq|“Asymptotic Symmetries and Memories of Gauge Theories in FLRW Spacetimes,”]] *Journal of High Energy Physics* **01** (2023) 011, [arXiv:2207.13726](https://arxiv.org/abs/2207.13726). Supply the conformal-coordinate gauge-theory setting in which trace-free radiation carries a conserved conformal charge; this does not identify cosmological redshift with dissipative leakage.

### Conformal scale and dilation covariance

- Sean Curry and A. Rod Gover, “An Introduction to Conformal Geometry and Tractor Calculus, with a View to Applications in General Relativity,” [arXiv:1412.7559](https://arxiv.org/abs/1412.7559). Supplies the conformal-density and scale-section calculus; it does not identify conformal weight with engineering dimension.
- Huzihiro Araki and Laszlo Zsido, “Extension of the Structure Theorem of Borchers and Its Application to Half-Sided Modular Inclusions,” *Reviews in Mathematical Physics* **17** (2005), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1142/S0129055X05002388), [arXiv:math/0412061](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0412061). Supplies the half-sided modular dilation-covariance setting whose positive translation generator has the no-gap spectral orbit.
- John C. Collins, Anthony Duncan, and Satish D. Joglekar, [[library/trace-and-dilatation-anomalies-in-gauge-theories/inq|“Trace and Dilatation Anomalies in Gauge Theories,”]] *Physical Review D* **16** (1977), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevD.16.438). Relates the gauge-theory trace anomaly to renormalization; the anomaly supplies no infrared coercivity estimate.

### QFT reconstruction and recovery

- Konrad Osterwalder and Robert Schrader, “Axioms for Euclidean Green's Functions,” *Communications in Mathematical Physics* **31** (1973), [Project Euclid](https://projecteuclid.org/journals/communications-in-mathematical-physics/volume-31/issue-2/Axioms-for-Euclidean-Greens-functions/cmp/1103858969.pdf), and “Axioms for Euclidean Green's Functions II,” *Communications in Mathematical Physics* **42** (1975), [Project Euclid](https://projecteuclid.org/journals/communications-in-mathematical-physics/volume-42/issue-3/Axioms-for-Euclidean-Greens-functions-II-with-an-Appendix-by/cmp/1103899050.pdf). Supply the reconstruction package; reflection positivity alone is only one premise.
- Abel Klein, [[library/the-semigroup-characterization-of-osterwalder-schrader-path-spaces/inq|“The Semigroup Characterization of Osterwalder--Schrader Path Spaces and the Construction of Euclidean Fields,”]] *Journal of Functional Analysis* **27** (1978), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1016/0022-1236(78)90009-5). Isolates the self-adjoint contraction semigroup obtained after OS quotienting; it does not identify an arbitrary one-sided semigroup with time translation.
- Martin Lüscher, [[library/construction-of-a-selfadjoint-strictly-positive-transfer-matrix-for-euclidean-lattice-gauge-theories/inq|“Construction of a Selfadjoint, Strictly Positive Transfer Matrix for Euclidean Lattice Gauge Theories,”]] *Communications in Mathematical Physics* **54** (1977), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01614090). Establishes the additional transfer-operator positivity needed to define a finite-lattice Hamiltonian by a logarithm.
- Dirk Schlingemann, [[library/from-euclidean-field-theory-to-quantum-field-theory/inq|“From Euclidean Field Theory to Quantum Field Theory,”]] *Reviews in Mathematical Physics* **11** (1999), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1142/S0129055X99000362), [arXiv:hep-th/9802035](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/9802035). Gives an algebraic reconstruction from a Euclidean local net under a time-zero condition; the Euclidean locality input is not manufactured by reflection positivity.
- Palle Jorgensen and Feng Tian, [[library/reflection-positivity-and-spectral-theory/inq|“Reflection Positivity and Spectral Theory,”]] [arXiv:1705.05262](https://arxiv.org/abs/1705.05262). Formulate reflection positivity and its stronger Markov counterpart through projections on a common Hilbert carrier; they supply source context for the projection geometry, while the separated past--future angle theorem here is a new deduction rather than a result attributed to them.
- Detlev Buchholz and Rainer Verch, “Scaling Algebras and Renormalization Group in Algebraic Quantum Field Theory,” *Reviews in Mathematical Physics* **7** (1995), [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/9501063). Constructs a canonical scaling algebra for local observable nets and intrinsic scaling limits.
- Detlev Buchholz and Rainer Verch, [[library/scaling-algebras-and-renormalization-group-in-algebraic-quantum-field-theory-ii-instructive-examples/inq|“Scaling Algebras and Renormalization Group in Algebraic Quantum Field Theory II: Instructive Examples,”]] *Reviews in Mathematical Physics* **10** (1998), [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/9708095). Shows that massive free fields in two and three spatial dimensions have massless short-distance scaling limits; ultraviolet scale structure need not retain an infrared gap.
- Dirk Schlingemann, [[library/short-distance-analysis-for-algebraic-euclidean-field-theory/inq|“Short-Distance Analysis for Algebraic Euclidean Field Theory,”]] [arXiv:hep-th/9907167](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/9907167). Under its algebraic Euclidean hypotheses, proves that reconstruction of a Euclidean scaling limit gives a net equivalent to a scaling-limit net of the reconstructed Minkowski theory; it presupposes the Euclidean net and scaling limit.
- Martin Luscher, [[library/properties-and-uses-of-the-wilson-flow-in-lattice-qcd/inq|“Properties and Uses of the Wilson Flow in Lattice QCD,”]] *Journal of High Energy Physics* **08** (2010) 071, [arXiv:1006.4518](https://arxiv.org/abs/1006.4518). Supplies gauge-covariant smoothing at radius of order \(\sqrt{8t}\); on a finite lattice the flow is an invertible field-space diffeomorphism, not a forgetful descent.
- Andreas Axelsson, Stephen Keith, and Alan McIntosh, [[library/quadratic-estimates-and-functional-calculi-of-perturbed-dirac-operators/inq|“Quadratic Estimates and Functional Calculi of Perturbed Dirac Operators,”]] *Inventiones Mathematicae* **163** (2006), [arXiv:math/0412321](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0412321). Supplies a broader quadratic-estimate and functional-calculus context; the exact resolvent-logistic transform used here is a direct self-adjoint spectral-theorem specialization.
- Pascal Auscher, Alan McIntosh, and Andrew J. Morris, [[library/calderon-reproducing-formulas-and-applications-to-hardy-spaces/inq|“Calderon Reproducing Formulas and Applications to Hardy Spaces,”]] *Revista Matematica Iberoamericana* **31** (2015), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.4171/RMI/857), [arXiv:1304.0168](https://arxiv.org/abs/1304.0168). Supplies the closest general self-adjoint functional-calculus precedent for the scale analysis/synthesis form and its localization obligations.
- Romeo Brunetti, Klaus Fredenhagen, and Rainer Verch, “The Generally Covariant Locality Principle,” *Communications in Mathematical Physics* **237** (2003), [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/math-ph/0112041). Formulates locally covariant QFT as a covariant functor with fields as natural transformations.
- David B. Malament, “The Class of Continuous Timelike Curves Determines the Topology of Spacetime,” *Journal of Mathematical Physics* **18** (1977), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1063/1.523436). Under the distinguishing hypotheses, supplies the causal reconstruction result used to separate conformal causal structure from the missing metric scale.

- BIPM, [“SI base unit: metre,”](https://www.bipm.org/en/si-base-units/metre) undated current institutional page, accessed 2026-09-04. Direct HTML confirms that the metre calibration uses both the fixed numerical value of \(c\) and the second defined through the caesium frequency. Supports the metrological example only, not an ontological claim that length requires light signals.
- Vivek Iyer and Robert M. Wald, [[library/some-properties-of-noether-charge-and-a-proposal-for-dynamical-black-hole-entropy/inq|“Some Properties of Noether Charge and a Proposal for Dynamical Black Hole Entropy,”]] *Physical Review D* **50** (1994), 846--864, [primary HTML](https://arxiv.org/html/gr-qc/9403028v1), accessed 2026-09-04. Equations (118) and (124) respectively give the Einstein area law and a curvature-dependent Lovelock example. Supports theory dependence of the entropy functional; it does not derive the proposed Yang--Mills comparison.

## Source exclusions and caution

- Sweeping recent solution claims were used only for landscape and theorem-architecture comparison. Publication and an asserted match to the Clay axioms were not treated as validation of the proof.
- Numerical glueball masses and the $46$–$47\,\mathrm{MeV}$ coincidence were treated as diagnostics, not proof premises.
- Entropic-gravity papers were not used as a Yang--Mills yardstick: their area coefficients or Newton coupling are imported or separately postulated, and no full-carrier Yang--Mills coercivity theorem follows.
- A Type III designation, modular spectrum, KMS normalization, or Connes trace-scaling character was not treated as physical time or energy without a correspondence map.
- The conditional real/complex/quaternionic pincer in the raw exploration inbox/supplying-complex-numbers/the-necessity-of-i.md was not treated as a uniqueness theorem for physical complex numbers; codimension-two monodromy can supply a phase slot without choosing \(i\) over \(-i\), a canonical \(U(1)\) character, or a positive modulus.
- The WKB envelope in [[inbox/the-box-spectrum-functor/the-box-spectrum-functor]] was not treated as a Born-dwell theorem; the physical Hilbert measure and symmetric factor ordering must be fixed first.

## Resulting construction target

The research supports the following conditional lower-bound schema:

$$
\Delta_E
\geq
\underbrace{\kappa_{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\text{dimensionless causal completeness}}
\underbrace{\eta_{\mathrm{sol}}}_{\text{dimensionless energy comparison}}
\underbrace{E_*}_{\text{independently calibrated energy scale}}.
$$

The strongest currently realized member on a Yang--Mills physical carrier is finite lattice Yang--Mills, where the continuous-time Kogut--Susskind physical form is exactly a vacuum-weighted flux Dirichlet form. On the unweighted pure-gauge Haar carrier of a connected graph containing a cycle, the exact lower frame constant is $g(\Gamma)\lambda_G$. The exceptional holonomy branch lands on that same configuration carrier: its normal character and trace metric give \(H_N(\kappa_N,\lambda_N)=H_W(\kappa_N/8,288\lambda_N)\), and the normalization-free unknown is the relative vacuum coercivity \(\Theta_\Gamma\), not the isolated factor \(8\). Euclidean time-slice sewing instead constructs the vacuum law for the finite-spacing Wilson transfer Hamiltonian. These two Hamiltonians meet only after a controlled temporal-continuum limit; at fixed spacing the hoped-for unbounded domination of the transfer logarithm by the group Laplacian is false. The positive replacement is now exact on the pure kinetic carrier: for normalized \(SU(2)\) Wilson convolution the first nontrivial Bessel ratio \(r_x=I_2(x)/I_1(x)\) controls the whole bounded defect, gauge closure raises the contraction to \(r_x^{g(\Gamma)}\), and the logarithmic temporal rate converges to the electric girth--Casimir normalization. The full finite Wilson sandwich also has an exact interacting edge after ground-state transformation, but the explicit global-extrema certificate decays exponentially with volume on regular fixed-coupling lattice families. A concrete next route is a local or block-conditional stationary two-slice comparison, which tensorizes without multiplying one loss factor per link and survives gauge restriction and deterministic one-step blocking; the calculation does not exclude every possible global comparison. Two broader noncircular routes remain: prove uniform local conditional coercivity plus subcritical influence for $\psi_{0,a,L}^2\mu_{a,L}$, or prove a uniform bulk Euclidean functional inequality and pass it directly to the time-zero marginal. The finite-transfer route must use a bounded one-step solder and its exact logarithmic rate; the continuous-time route may use an unbounded common-form comparison. Either route still requires survival of the calibrated product in the continuum. A separately constructed causal relaxation carrier has its own exact comparison theorem: causal coercivity $\kappa_C$, lower carrier coverage $b_J$, an energy solder $\eta_{\mathrm{sol}}$, and a yardstick $E_*$ imply $\Delta_E\geq\eta_{\mathrm{sol}}\kappa_Cb_JE_*$. Type III/core and Hessian machinery are comparison tools for these constructions, not substitutes for them.

The exact finite-regulator bottleneck can now be stated without any symmetry-breaking language. Let \(T_r\) be the strictly positive Wilson transfer operator on the reconstructed slice carrier, let \(\lambda_{0,r}\) be its vacuum eigenvalue, and let the ground-state transform put

$$
P_{T,r}
:=
U_r^{-1}\frac{T_r}{\lambda_{0,r}}U_r,
\qquad
H_{T,r}-E_{0,r}
=
-\frac{\hbar c}{a_{\tau,r}}\,U_r(\log P_{T,r})U_r^{-1}.
$$

Then \(P_{T,r}\) is a reversible one-step Markov operator under the kernel-positivity hypotheses, and functional calculus gives the exact same-carrier inequality

$$
\left\langle f,-\log P_{T,r}f\right\rangle
\geq
\left\langle f,(I-P_{T,r})f\right\rangle.
$$

For the pure kinetic \(SU(2)\) Wilson transfer this bounded problem is solved exactly, not merely estimated. Normalized central convolution has Peter--Weyl eigenvalues

$$
p_j(x)=\frac{I_{2j+1}(x)}{I_1(x)},
\qquad
1=p_0>p_{1/2}>p_1>\cdots>0.
$$

Soni's strict order monotonicity and Näsell's ratio bound therefore give

$$
I-P_x
\geq
\left(1-\frac{I_2(x)}{I_1(x)}\right)(I-\Pi_{\mathbf1}),
\qquad
